output
1	# RL Lab — Spec
2	
3	**Status:** Approved · **Last updated:** 2026-10-19 · **Brief:** [brief.md](brief.md)
4	
5	## Summary
6	A lab of small 2D game environments where hand-written reinforcement-learning agents learn by trial and error, all on one shared core. The first stage builds classic Snake and a hand-written value-learning agent that sees 11 yes/no facts. The second builds a circular fish tank, where several fish share one policy, see only through 8 short rays and learn continuous steering away from a bouncing ball, through a hand-written ladder of three policy-gradient agents. The third turns the tank into predator-prey foraging: one predator hunts prey that must eat pellets to survive, both sides learn with the hand-written limited-update agent, and a ladder of taking turns, learning at once and self-play against past versions shows why training against past versions is needed, judged by a cross-play table of every generation against every other. Every environment has random and scripted baselines scored on the same games, learning curves, a replay viewer and a notes page; Snake and the fish tank also have switches that turn parts of the agent off. See the [brief](brief.md) for why.
7	
8	## Users
9	| User | What they need to do |
10	|---|---|
11	| The learner (sole user) | Train an agent, or two sides against each other, on an environment, compare it with baselines, with the rung below it, with its own ablated versions and with its own past generations, watch any agent or pair of sides play, and write down why it learns |
12	
13	## Scope
14	**In scope:** Snake environment, fish tank environment, predator-prey tank, shared core, value-learning agent, policy-gradient ladder, self-play ladder, cross-play table, ablation switches, baselines, evaluation, run logging, learning curves, replay viewer, explanation notes, the optional discrete tank.
15	
16	**Out of scope:**
17	- Learning from raw pixels or the full grid or tank image; Snake's agent sees only the 11 hand-picked facts and each fish only its rays.
18	- Comparing against library implementations of the same algorithms (a later step).
19	- Views beyond learning curves, replays and the cross-play table: no heatmaps of the agent's values, no dashboards, no other visualisations.
20	- Real environments beyond predator-prey foraging; the next one is chosen once it is done. Beyond Snake, the fish tank and the predator-prey tank, the core is tested only with tiny throwaway test environments.
21	- Mastering Snake: the agent sees only nearby danger and may trap itself; no requirement asks it to fill the board.
22	- Fish that crowd, block or push each other; prey interact only by eating pellets another prey could have eaten.
23	- A curriculum of harder tanks: no speeding ball or predator, no extra balls, no more than one predator.
24	- Evolutionary methods: no population of agents where the fittest are kept.
25	- Learned communication or teaming among prey: they share one policy, nothing more.
26	- Ablation switches in the predator-prey tank: its self-play ladder is the comparison.
27	- GPUs, paid tools and paid services.
28	
29	## Features
30	
31	### Snake environment (`SNAKE`)
32	**Serves:** Goal 2, Goal 3
33	**Stage:** S1
34	**Behavior:** Classic Snake on a 20 × 20 grid. The snake starts 3 cells long in the middle of the grid, heading right, with one food on a random empty cell. Each step the agent picks one of 3 moves relative to its heading: straight, turn left, turn right. Eating food grows the snake by 1 and places new food; hitting a wall or its own body ends the game. The score of a game is the number of food eaten.
35	
36	**Requirements**
37	- **SNAKE-01** The game is played on a 20 × 20 grid with a snake that starts 3 cells long in the middle, heading right.
38	  - Given a new game, when it starts, then the snake occupies 3 adjacent cells in the middle of the grid, heading right, and exactly one food is on a cell the snake does not occupy.
39	- **SNAKE-02** Each step the agent chooses one of 3 moves relative to the snake's heading: straight, turn left or turn right.
40	  - Given the snake heading up, when the agent picks "turn left", then the head moves one cell left and the heading becomes left.
41	  - Given any heading, when any of the 3 moves is picked, then the snake never reverses into its own neck.
42	- **SNAKE-03** Moving the head onto the food grows the snake by 1 cell, adds 1 to the score and places new food on a random empty cell.
43	  - Given food directly ahead, when the agent moves straight, then the snake is 1 cell longer, the score rises by 1 and a new food appears on a cell the snake does not occupy.
44	- **SNAKE-04** Moving the head into a wall or into the snake's own body ends the game.
45	  - Given the head next to the right wall heading right, when the agent moves straight, then the game ends with its current score.
46	  - Given the head beside its own body, when the agent turns into it, then the game ends.
47	- **SNAKE-05** A game also ends when the snake goes 100 × its length steps without eating.
48	  - Given a snake of length 5 that has not eaten for 500 steps, when it takes another step without eating, then the game ends with its current score.
49	- **SNAKE-06** The agent observes exactly 11 yes/no facts each step: danger straight ahead, danger to the left, danger to the right (danger = wall or body in the adjacent cell); heading up, down, left, right; food is up, down, left, right of the head.
50	  - Given the head heading right with the wall in the next cell and food above-left, when the agent observes, then "danger straight", "heading right", "food up" and "food left" are yes and the other 7 facts are no.
51	- **SNAKE-07** Rewards are +10 for eating food, −10 for the game ending by collision or step limit, and 0 for every other step.
52	  - Given a step that eats food, when the reward is reported, then it is +10; given a step that ends the game, then it is −10; given any other step, then it is 0.
53	- **SNAKE-08** A game started with the same seed and played with the same moves unfolds identically, including every food position.
54	  - Given seed 7 and a fixed list of moves, when the game is played twice, then every frame, reward and the final score are identical.
55	
56	**Edge cases & errors**
57	- The snake fills every cell → the game ends as a win with its score; no food is placed.
58	- Food would be placed on the snake → it is placed only on empty cells.
59	- The agent sends a move other than the 3 allowed → the step is refused with an error naming the allowed moves; the game is unchanged.
60	- A step is requested after the game has ended → refused with an error saying the game is over.
61	
62	### Fish tank environment (`TANK`)
63	**Serves:** Goal 2, Goal 3
64	**Stage:** S2
65	**Source:** [ideas/fish-tank.md](ideas/fish-tank.md)
66	**Behavior:** A circular tank with a ball bouncing around inside it and several fish swimming in it. Movement is continuous: each step every living fish chooses how far to turn and how fast to swim. Each fish sees only nearby things, through 8 short sensor rays. When the ball touches a fish, that fish is removed; the game ends when every fish is gone or the time limit is reached. Fish earn a reward for every step they stay alive, so a good fish survives as long as possible. The fish never affect each other.
67	
68	**Requirements**
69	- **TANK-01** The tank is a circle of radius 10 units holding one ball of radius 1 that moves at a constant 0.25 units per step and bounces off the wall, leaving it at the same angle it arrived.
70	  - Given the ball moving straight out along a radius, when it reaches the wall, then it comes back along the same line in the opposite direction, still at 0.25 units per step.
71	  - Given the ball arriving at the wall at 30° to it, when it bounces, then it leaves at 30° on the other side of the line to the centre.
72	- **TANK-02** Each step every living fish chooses two continuous controls: a turn of −15° to +15° and a speed of 0 to 0.3 units per step; the fish turns first, then swims forward.
73	  - Given a fish at the centre heading east, when it chooses turn 0° and speed 0.3, then it ends 0.3 units east of the centre, still heading east.
74	  - Given the same fish, when it chooses turn +15° and speed 0, then it stays in place and its heading turns 15° anticlockwise.
75	- **TANK-03** A game holds a set number of fish, from 1 to 10 (5 unless set otherwise); the ball starts at the centre in a random direction, and each fish starts at a random spot at least 3 units from the ball, with a random heading.
76	  - Given 5 fish and seed 2, when a game starts, then 5 fish are inside the tank, each at least 3 units from the ball.
77	  - Given 1 fish, when a game is played, then it runs exactly as a several-fish game does, with one fish.
78	- **TANK-04** Fish never affect each other: they pass through one another, and no fish's move changes another fish's position.
79	  - Given two fish on the same spot, when both move, then each ends exactly where its own move would take it if it were alone.
80	- **TANK-05** A fish the ball touches (centres closer than 1.3 units: ball radius 1 plus fish radius 0.3) is removed, and the episode ends when all are gone.
81	  - Given a fish whose centre is 1.2 units from the ball's centre after a step, when the step is reported, then that fish is removed and is no longer asked for moves.
82	  - Given the last living fish is removed, when the step is reported, then the game has ended.
83	- **TANK-06** A game also ends after 1,000 steps, with every fish still alive counted as surviving all 1,000.
84	  - Given 2 fish alive at step 1,000, when the step is reported, then the game has ended and both fish survived 1,000 steps.
85	- **TANK-07** Each fish sees through 8 rays spread evenly around its heading, 45° apart, each reaching 4 units. Each ray reports the distance to the wall, to the ball and to the nearest other fish along it, each scaled from 0 (touching) to 1 (nothing within reach); the fish also senses its own current speed, scaled 0 to 1. A fish's observation is these 25 numbers.
86	  - Given a fish heading east with the ball's edge 2 units straight ahead and nothing else within 4 units, when it observes, then the forward ray's ball reading is 0.5 and every other wall, ball and fish reading is 1.
87	  - Given the ball 6 units away, when the fish observes, then every ball reading is 1.
88	  - Given two games identical except for the ball's direction of motion, when a fish observes, then its observations are identical: a fish doesn't sense the ball's motion.
89	- **TANK-08** A fish that swims into the wall stops at the wall, with no other effect.
90	  - Given a fish 0.1 units from the wall heading straight at it, when it swims at speed 0.3, then it ends touching the wall, inside the tank, with its heading unchanged.
91	- **TANK-09** Each fish earns +1 for every step it survives and 0 on the step it is removed; a game's score is the average number of steps its fish survived.
92	  - Given 2 fish where one is removed at step 100 and the other survives to step 1,000, when the game ends, then its score is 550.
93	- **TANK-10** A game started with the same seed and played with the same moves unfolds identically, including every ball and fish position.
94	  - Given seed 7 and a fixed list of moves, when the game is played twice, then every frame, reward and the final score are identical.
95	
96	**Edge cases & errors**
97	- A move outside a control's limits → the step is refused with an error naming the control and its limits; the game is unchanged.
98	- A move is sent for a removed fish, or a living fish has no move → the step is refused with an error naming the fish.
99	- A step is requested after the game has ended → refused with an error saying the game is over.
100	- Two fish are removed on the same step → both are removed; if no fish is left, the game ends on that step.
101	- The ball touches a fish on the step the time limit is reached → the fish is removed first, so it survived 999 steps.
102	
103	### Predator-prey tank (`HUNT`)
104	**Serves:** Goal 2, Goal 3
105	**Stage:** S3
106	**Source:** [ideas/predator-prey.md](ideas/predator-prey.md)
107	**Behavior:** The fish tank with stakes. The bouncing ball is replaced by one predator fish, and food pellets are scattered around the tank. Prey lose energy every step and must eat pellets to keep it up, so a prey that only hides starves. A prey caught by the predator, or out of energy, is removed; the game ends when every prey is gone or the time limit is reached. Prey earn a reward for every step they stay alive; the predator earns one for every prey it catches. Both sides see only nearby things through rays. Each step runs in this order: every fish turns and swims, the predator catches, prey eat, then every prey loses energy.
108	
109	**Requirements**
110	- **HUNT-01** The tank is a circle of radius 10 units holding one predator, a set number of prey from 1 to 10 (5 unless set otherwise) and 10 food pellets. The predator starts at the centre with a random heading; each prey starts at a random spot at least 3 units from the predator, with a random heading; each pellet starts at a random spot.
111	  - Given 5 prey and seed 2, when a game starts, then 1 predator, 5 prey and 10 pellets are inside the tank, and each prey is at least 3 units from the predator.
112	- **HUNT-02** Each step every living prey chooses a turn of −15° to +15° and a speed of 0 to 0.3 units per step, and the predator chooses a turn of −10° to +10° and a speed of 0 to 0.35 units per step; each fish turns first, then swims forward, and stops at the wall if it swims into it.
113	  - Given the predator at the centre heading east, when it chooses turn 0° and speed 0.35, then it ends 0.35 units east of the centre, still heading east.
114	  - Given a prey 0.1 units from the wall heading straight at it, when it swims at speed 0.3, then it ends touching the wall, inside the tank, with its heading unchanged.
115	- **HUNT-03** No fish's move changes another fish's position: prey pass through each other and through the predator.
116	  - Given two prey on the same spot, when both move, then each ends exactly where its own move would take it if it were alone.
117	- **HUNT-04** The predator (radius 0.5) catches every prey whose centre is closer than 0.8 units to its own (predator radius 0.5 plus prey radius 0.3) after the step's moves; a caught prey is removed.
118	  - Given a prey whose centre is 0.7 units from the predator's after a step, when the step is reported, then that prey is removed and is no longer asked for moves.
119	- **HUNT-05** Each prey starts with energy 1.0 and loses 0.002 at the end of every step; a prey whose energy reaches 0 starves and is removed.
120	  - Given a prey that never eats, when step 500 is reported, then its energy is 0 and it is removed.
121	- **HUNT-06** A prey whose centre comes within 0.5 units of a pellet (pellet radius 0.2 plus prey radius 0.3) eats it: its energy rises by 0.3, up to at most 1.0, the pellet disappears and a new pellet appears at a random spot, so 10 pellets are always in the tank.
122	  - Given a prey with energy 0.5 that ends its move 0.4 units from a pellet, when the step is reported, then its energy is 0.798 (0.5 + 0.3 − 0.002), that pellet is gone and 10 pellets are in the tank.
123	  - Given a prey with energy 0.9 that eats a pellet, when the step is reported, then its energy is 0.998 (capped at 1.0, then less 0.002).
124	- **HUNT-07** A game ends when every prey is gone or after 1,000 steps, with every prey still alive counted as surviving all 1,000.
125	  - Given 2 prey alive at step 1,000, when the step is reported, then the game has ended and both prey survived 1,000 steps.
126	- **HUNT-08** Each prey sees through 8 rays spread evenly around its heading, 45° apart, each reaching 4 units. Each ray reports the distance to the wall, to the predator, to the nearest other prey and to the nearest pellet along it, each scaled from 0 (touching) to 1 (nothing within reach); the prey also senses its own current speed, scaled 0 to 1, and its own energy. A prey's observation is these 34 numbers.
127	  - Given a prey heading east with energy 0.6, a pellet's edge 2 units straight ahead and nothing else within 4 units, when it observes, then the forward ray's pellet reading is 0.5, its energy reading is 0.6, and every other wall, predator, prey and pellet reading is 1.
128	- **HUNT-09** The predator sees through 8 rays spread the same way, each reaching 4 units, each reporting the distance to the wall and to the nearest prey along it, scaled from 0 to 1; it also senses its own current speed, scaled 0 to 1. The predator's observation is these 17 numbers.
129	  - Given the predator heading east with a prey's edge 2 units straight ahead and nothing else within 4 units, when it observes, then the forward ray's prey reading is 0.5 and every other wall and prey reading is 1.
130	- **HUNT-10** Each prey earns +1 for every step it survives and 0 on the step it is removed; the prey's score for a game is the average number of steps its prey survived.
131	  - Given 2 prey where one is caught at step 100 and the other survives to step 1,000, when the game ends, then the prey's score is 550.
132	- **HUNT-11** The predator earns +1 on each step for every prey it catches on that step, and 0 otherwise; the predator's score for a game is the number of prey it caught.
133	  - Given a step on which the predator catches 2 prey, when the step is reported, then its reward is +2.
134	  - Given 5 prey where the predator catches 3 and 2 starve, when the game ends, then the predator's score is 3.
135	- **HUNT-12** A game started with the same seed and played with the same moves unfolds identically, including every predator, prey and pellet position.
136	  - Given seed 7 and a fixed list of moves for both sides, when the game is played twice, then every frame, reward and both final scores are identical.
137	
138	**Edge cases & errors**
139	- A move outside a control's limits → the step is refused with an error naming the side, the control and its limits; the game is unchanged.
140	- A move is sent for a removed prey, or a living prey or the predator has no move → the step is refused with an error naming the fish.
141	- A step is requested after the game has ended → refused with an error saying the game is over.
142	- A prey is caught on the step it would eat a pellet or starve → it is caught: the predator earns +1 and the pellet stays.
143	- Two prey reach the same pellet on the same step → the prey whose centre is closer eats it; on an exact tie, the one listed first; the other gains nothing.
144	- A new pellet would appear within 0.5 units of a living prey → it is placed elsewhere, so no pellet is eaten on the step it appears.
145	- A prey is removed on the step the time limit is reached → it is removed first, so it survived 999 steps.
146	
147	### Shared core (`CORE`)
148	**Serves:** Goal 4
149	**Stage:** S1
150	**Behavior:** Every environment offers the same small set of behaviors: start a game from a seed, take one move for each of its acting agents and report what happened to each, say how big an observation is and what moves are allowed, and draw one frame for the replay viewer. The training loop, logging, learning curves and replay viewer use only these behaviors, so a new environment plugs in without changing them, whether its moves are a few choices or continuous controls, whether it has one agent or several, and whether one side learns or two sides learn against each other. Growing the core leaves Snake and the fish tank running exactly as before.
151	
152	**Requirements**
153	- **CORE-01** Every environment can start a new game from a given seed and returns the first observation.
154	  - Given any environment and seed 3, when a game is started twice, then both first observations are identical.
155	- **CORE-02** Every environment takes one move for each of its acting agents (Snake: the snake; the tank: each living fish; the predator-prey tank: the predator and each living prey) and reports, for each, its next observation and reward, plus whether the game has ended.
156	  - Given a running Snake game, when a move is taken, then one next observation, one reward and the game-over flag are reported.
157	  - Given a tank game with 3 living fish, when 3 moves are taken, then 3 next observations, 3 rewards and the game-over flag are reported.
158	  - Given a predator-prey game with 4 living prey, when 5 moves are taken (the predator's and 4 prey's), then 5 next observations, 5 rewards and the game-over flag are reported.
159	- **CORE-03** Every environment reports, for each of its sides, its observation size and its allowed moves: either a number of choices, or a number of continuous controls with each control's limits.
160	  - Given Snake, when asked, then it reports 11 facts and 3 choices.
161	  - Given the tank, when asked, then it reports 25 numbers and 2 controls: turn −15° to +15°, speed 0 to 0.3.
162	  - Given the predator-prey tank, when asked, then it reports two sides: the prey with 34 numbers and 2 controls (turn −15° to +15°, speed 0 to 0.3), and the predator with 17 numbers and 2 controls (turn −10° to +10°, speed 0 to 0.35).
163	- **CORE-04** Every environment draws a frame of its current state for the replay viewer.
164	  - Given a running Snake game, when a frame is requested, then it shows the grid, the snake, the food, the score and the step number.
165	  - Given a running tank game, when a frame is requested, then it shows the tank, the ball, each living fish with its heading, the number of fish left and the step number.
166	  - Given a running predator-prey game, when a frame is requested, then it shows the tank, the pellets, the predator with its heading, each living prey with its heading and energy, the number of prey left and the step number.
167	- **CORE-05** The training loop trains any agent on any environment that offers CORE-01 to CORE-04, for a set number of games, with a given seed.
168	  - Given a tiny test environment (a 1 × 5 corridor where reaching the right end gives +1), when the training loop runs on it with no changes to the core, then training completes and the agent reaches the right end in ≥ 9 of 10 evaluation games.
169	- **CORE-06** Logging, learning curves and the replay viewer work on any environment that offers CORE-01 to CORE-04.
170	  - Given the tiny test environment, when a run is logged, its learning curve drawn and a game replayed, then each works with no changes to the core.
171	- **CORE-07** The training loop, logging, learning curves and replay viewer work on an environment with continuous controls and several agents at once.
172	  - Given a tiny continuous test environment (2 points on a line from 0 to 5, each pushing itself −1 to +1 per step, +1 for reaching 5), when a policy-gradient agent trains on it with no changes to the core, then training completes, both points reach 5 in ≥ 9 of 10 evaluation games, and the run is logged, drawn and replayed.
173	- **CORE-08** When an environment removes one of its agents mid-game, the training loop stops asking that agent for moves and the game continues for the rest.
174	  - Given the continuous test environment where one point reaches 5 first, when the next step is taken, then only the other point is asked for a move.
175	- **CORE-09** Growing the core leaves Snake's and the fish tank's results unchanged.
176	  - Given a 200-game Snake run with seed 1 recorded before the core grew, when it is repeated after, then every logged evaluation score is identical.
177	  - Given a 200-game rung-3 tank run with seed 1 recorded before the core grew, when it is repeated after, then every logged evaluation score is identical.
178	- **CORE-10** The training loop trains an environment with two sides, each with its own agent, and each side either learns or plays frozen, without learning, in any game.
179	  - Given a tiny two-sided test environment (a chaser and a runner on a line from 0 to 5, each pushing itself −1 to +1 per step; the chaser earns +1 for touching the runner, the runner +1 for every step untouched), when both sides train with no changes to the core, then training completes, both sides' policies change, and the run is logged, drawn and replayed.
180	  - Given the same environment with the runner frozen, when a training game ends, then the chaser's policy is updated and the runner's is identical to before the game.
181	
182	**Edge cases & errors**
183	- An environment is missing one of the 4 behaviors → the core refuses it at start-up and names the missing behavior.
184	- An agent returns a move outside the environment's allowed choices or limits → the run stops with an error naming the move and the game step.
185	- An agent built for choices is given an environment with continuous controls, or the other way round → refused at start-up, naming both kinds.
186	- A two-sided environment is started with an agent for only one side → refused at start-up, naming the side with no agent.
187	
188	### Value-learning agent (`AGENT`)
189	**Serves:** Goal 1, Goal 2
190	**Stage:** S1
191	**Behavior:** The agent learns an estimate of each move's long-run value from the 11 facts, using a small learned function. It picks moves by mostly taking the best-valued move and sometimes a random one, with the random share shrinking over training. It learns from random batches of remembered past steps, and builds its targets from a periodically refreshed frozen copy of its value estimate. Every part is written by hand.
192	
193	**Requirements**
194	- **AGENT-01** The agent estimates, for each observation, a value for every allowed move.
195	  - Given an observation of 11 facts, when the agent is asked, then it returns 3 values, one per move.
196	- **AGENT-02** During training the agent takes a random move with a probability that starts at 1.0 and decays to 0.01 over the first half of the training games, and otherwise takes its best-valued move.
197	  - Given training game 0, when the agent picks moves, then every move is random; given a game in the second half, then about 1% of moves are random.
198	- **AGENT-03** During evaluation the agent always takes its best-valued move.
199	  - Given an evaluation game, when the agent picks a move, then it is the move with the highest value, and the same observation always gives the same move.
200	- **AGENT-04** The agent remembers its most recent 100,000 steps and learns from random batches of them.
201	  - Given more than 100,000 steps taken, when the agent learns, then its batches come only from the latest 100,000 steps, drawn at random.
202	- **AGENT-05** The agent's learning targets are the step's reward plus the discounted best value of the next observation (discount 0.9), taken from a frozen copy of its value estimate that is refreshed every 1,000 steps; a game-ending step's target is its reward alone.
203	  - Given a non-final step with reward 0, when its target is built, then it equals 0.9 × the frozen copy's best value for the next observation.
204	  - Given a game-ending step with reward −10, when its target is built, then it is −10.
205	  - Given 1,000 learning steps since the last refresh, when the next one happens, then the frozen copy is replaced by the current estimate.
206	- **AGENT-06** A trained agent can be saved and loaded back, and the loaded agent picks the same moves.
207	  - Given a saved agent, when it is loaded and plays an evaluation game with seed 11, then it makes exactly the same moves as before saving.
208	
209	**Edge cases & errors**
210	- Fewer remembered steps than one batch → the agent keeps playing and does not learn until it has a full batch.
211	- A saved agent is loaded for an environment with a different observation size or move count → loading is refused with an error naming both sizes.
212	- Learning produces a non-number value → the run stops with an error naming the game and step, and the log so far is kept.
213	
214	### Policy-gradient ladder (`PG`)
215	**Serves:** Goal 1, Goal 2
216	**Stage:** S2
217	**Source:** [ideas/fish-tank.md](ideas/fish-tank.md)
218	**Behavior:** Three hand-written agents learn the fish's steering policy directly, each one built on the one before and each fixing a failure of the rung below. Rung 1, plain policy gradient, makes moves that were followed by high returns more likely. Rung 2, actor-critic, also learns a value estimate and credits each move only with how much better it did than expected, which cuts the noise. Rung 3, the limited-update agent, reuses each game's steps several times but caps how far each update can move the policy, so reuse doesn't wreck it. All fish in a game share one policy, each acting on its own observation. Each rung is compared with the rung below it.
219	
220	**Requirements**
221	- **PG-01** Every rung's policy gives, for each observation, a spread of likely moves for each control (a centre and a width), and during training each move is drawn from it.
222	  - Given the same observation twice during training, when moves are drawn, then they can differ, and over 1,000 draws their average is within 0.05 of the policy's centre for each control.
223	- **PG-02** During evaluation every rung takes the centre of its policy for each control.
224	  - Given an evaluation game, when the agent picks a move, then the same observation always gives the same move.
225	- **PG-03** Every move a rung sends lies within the environment's limits, even when the drawn move falls outside them.
226	  - Given a drawn turn of +20°, when it is sent to the tank, then the tank receives +15°.
227	- **PG-04** All fish of one side in a game share one policy (in the fish tank, every fish; in the predator-prey tank, every prey): each fish acts on its own observation, and every fish's steps feed that side's updates.
228	  - Given a game with 5 fish, when the agent learns from it, then one policy is updated, from the steps of all 5 fish.
229	  - Given a predator-prey game with 5 prey, when both sides learn from it, then the prey's one policy is updated from the steps of all 5 prey, and the predator's policy from the predator's steps alone.
230	- **PG-05** Every rung learns once after each training game, from all the steps of all its fish, with discount 0.99.
231	  - Given a training game of 3 fish surviving 40, 70 and 120 steps, when the game ends, then the agent makes one learning update from those 230 fish-steps.
232	- **PG-06** Rung 1, plain policy gradient, makes each move more or less likely in proportion to the discounted return that followed it, up to that fish's end.
233	  - Given a fish whose rewards were +1, +1, 0 before it was removed, when its steps are weighted, then the weights are 1.99, 1 and 0.
234	- **PG-07** Rung 2, actor-critic, learns a value estimate of each observation and weights each move by its advantage: how much better things went than the estimate expected, blending one-step and longer look-aheads with a blend factor of 0.95.
235	  - Given a fish's last step before removal with reward 0 and a value estimate of 2, when its advantage is built, then it is −2.
236	  - Given the value estimate after learning from many games, when it is checked on 100 held-out games, then it predicts the discounted return better than predicting the average return for every step.
237	- **PG-08** Rung 3, the limited-update agent, learns like rung 2 but reuses each game's steps for 10 passes in random batches of 64, and stops rewarding any move whose probability has already moved more than 20% up or down from the policy that played the game.
238	  - Given a move whose probability under the current policy is 1.3 × its probability when played, with a positive advantage, when the next pass updates, then that move gains nothing from becoming more likely.
239	- **PG-09** Rungs 2 and 3 rescale each game's advantages to an average of 0 and a spread of 1 before learning.
240	  - Given a game's advantages, when they are rescaled, then their mean is 0 and their standard deviation is 1, to within 0.001.
241	- **PG-10** Each rung's final evaluation scores are compared with the rung below it with the `null-hypothesis-testing` skill, given 5 training seeds × the same 100 held-out games for each rung; a rung counts as better only if its scores are higher at a 5% significance level after correcting for the 2 comparisons. The report also gives each rung's spread across its 5 seeds.
242	  - Given finished runs of all 3 rungs, when the ladder comparison runs, then the report says, for rung 2 against rung 1 and rung 3 against rung 2, "better" or "no detectable difference", with the test result and each rung's spread.
243	- **PG-11** A rung that is not better than the one below it is recorded as a finding in the notes, and S2 doesn't count as failed.
244	  - Given a ladder report where rung 3 shows "no detectable difference", when the stage is checked, then it passes, and the notes page records it as a finding (EXPL-04).
245	- **PG-12** A trained rung can be saved and loaded back, and the loaded agent picks the same moves.
246	  - Given a saved rung, when it is loaded and plays an evaluation game with seed 11, then it makes exactly the same moves as before saving.
247	
248	**Edge cases & errors**
249	- A game ends by the time limit → returns and advantages for the surviving fish stop at step 1,000; nothing is counted beyond it.
250	- A game where every fish is removed on the first step → the agent learns from those steps alone; it does not stop.
251	- A saved rung is loaded for an environment with a different observation size or controls → loading is refused with an error naming both.
252	- Learning produces a non-number value → the run stops with an error naming the game and step, and the log so far is kept.
253	
254	### Self-play ladder (`SELF`)
255	**Serves:** Goal 1, Goal 2
256	**Stage:** S3
257	**Source:** [ideas/predator-prey.md](ideas/predator-prey.md)
258	**Behavior:** In the predator-prey tank both sides learn, each with its own copy of the limited-update agent, so each side's world keeps changing as the other improves. A ladder of three ways to train them shows why: taking turns (optional) keeps each learner's world still; simultaneous learners, the broken version kept on purpose, chase a moving target; self-play against past versions, the fix, trains each side against a mix of its opponent's current and saved past generations. Every run saves generations of both sides as it goes, for the cross-play table.
259	
260	**Requirements**
261	- **SELF-01** Both sides are trained by the limited-update agent (PG-08), each side with its own policy: one shared by all prey, and one for the predator.
262	  - Given a predator-prey training run, when it starts, then two policies exist, one per side, and each learns only from its own side's steps.
263	- **SELF-02** Every predator-prey run saves a generation of each side at every tenth of training, 10 generations per side, the last at the end of training.
264	  - Given a finished run, when its saved generations are listed, then there are 10 predator generations and 10 prey generations, numbered 1 to 10 in the order they were saved.
265	- **SELF-03** In the taking-turns rung, training is split into 10 equal blocks that alternate sides, prey first; in each block one side learns while the other plays its latest policy frozen.
266	  - Given a taking-turns run, when block 1 ends, then the prey's policy has changed and the predator's is identical to its starting policy.
267	  - Given block 2, when it ends, then the predator's policy has changed and the prey's is identical to its policy at the end of block 1.
268	- **SELF-04** In the simultaneous rung, every training game pits the current predator against the current prey, and both sides learn after every game.
269	  - Given a simultaneous run, when any training game ends, then both sides' policies are updated from that game.
270	- **SELF-05** In the self-play rung, each training game is drawn as one of three kinds: current against current, both learning (50%); current predator against a past prey generation, only the predator learning (25%); a past predator generation against current prey, only the prey learning (25%). The past generation is drawn uniformly from the opponent's generations saved so far; until the first is saved, every game is current against current.
271	  - Given 10,000 self-play training games after the first generations are saved, when their kinds are counted, then 48–52% are current against current and 23–27% are each of the other two kinds.
272	  - Given 4 saved prey generations and 10,000 games against past prey, when the drawn generations are counted, then each was drawn in 23–27% of them.
273	  - Given a game of the current predator against a past prey generation, when it ends, then the predator's policy is updated and neither the current prey policy nor the saved generation changes.
274	- **SELF-06** The taking-turns rung is optional: S3 is complete with only the simultaneous and self-play rungs.
275	  - Given finished studies of the simultaneous and self-play rungs and no taking-turns runs, when the stage is checked, then it is complete, and every comparison and table leaves out the missing rung without counting it as failed.
276	- **SELF-07** A saved generation of either side can be loaded back, and the loaded generation picks the same moves.
277	  - Given a saved predator generation, when it is loaded and plays an evaluation game with seed 11 against the scripted prey, then it makes exactly the same moves as before saving.
278	- **SELF-08** A taking-turns or simultaneous rung that does not beat both baselines (EVAL-03), or whose later generations do not beat its earlier ones (CROSS-03), is recorded as a finding in the notes, and S3 doesn't count as failed.
279	  - Given a simultaneous rung whose last predator generation shows "no detectable difference" from its first, when the stage is checked, then it passes, and the notes page records it as a finding (EXPL-05).
280	
281	**Edge cases & errors**
282	- A game where every prey is removed on the first step → both learning sides learn from those steps alone; the run does not stop.
283	- A saved generation is loaded for the other side → loading is refused with an error naming both sides' observation sizes.
284	- A run stops before all 10 generations are saved → the saved ones are kept and the run is marked incomplete.
285	
286	### Cross-play table (`CROSS`)
287	**Serves:** Goal 1, Goal 2
288	**Stage:** S3
289	**Source:** [ideas/predator-prey.md](ideas/predator-prey.md)
290	**Behavior:** Each side's reward alone can't show learning, because its opponent improves too. The cross-play table pits every saved generation of the predator against every saved generation of the prey on the same held-out games, so the learner can see whether later generations beat earlier ones, which is real progress, or whether the two sides only chase each other in circles.
291	
292	**Requirements**
293	- **CROSS-01** For each finished predator-prey run, every predator generation plays every prey generation on the same 20 held-out games with exploration off, filling a 10 × 10 table of the predator's mean score and the prey's mean score for each pairing.
294	  - Given a finished run with 10 generations per side, when its table is built, then it has 100 cells, each holding both sides' mean scores over the same 20 held-out games.
295	- **CROSS-02** The table is shown with predator generations as rows and prey generations as columns, one table per rung averaged over its 5 training seeds, built from saved generations only, never by training again.
296	  - Given the 5 seeds of the self-play rung, when its table is shown, then each cell holds both sides' mean scores over the 5 seeds' tables.
297	- **CROSS-03** For each rung and each side, the last generation's scores are compared with the first generation's with the `null-hypothesis-testing` skill, given each generation's scores against all 10 opponent generations on the same 20 held-out games for each of the 5 training seeds; the later generation beats the earlier only if its scores are higher at a 5% significance level after correcting for every such comparison in the predator-prey tank (6: 3 rungs × 2 sides; 4 without the taking-turns rung).
298	  - Given finished runs of every rung, when the comparison runs, then the report says, for each rung's predator and each rung's prey, "later beats earlier" or "no detectable difference", with the test result.
299	- **CROSS-04** In the self-play rung, the last generation beats the first for both the predator and the prey.
300	  - Given the CROSS-03 report, when it is read, then the self-play rung's predator and prey each show "later beats earlier".
301	
302	**Edge cases & errors**
303	- A run has fewer than 10 generations saved → its table covers the saved ones, is marked incomplete, and the run is left out of the CROSS-03 comparison, which names it.
304	
305	### Ablation switches (`ABL`)
306	**Serves:** Goal 1
307	**Stage:** S1
308	**Behavior:** Snake and the fish tank each have three switches, each turning off one part of its top agent, so the learner can train the agent without it and compare. On Snake they turn off the value-learning agent's memory, frozen copy and exploration decay; on the tank they turn off the limited-update agent's value estimate, update cap and advantage rescaling. Each switch is set per run and recorded in the run's log. The comparison of each ablated agent with the full agent is judged by a hypothesis test.
309	
310	**Requirements**
311	- **ABL-01** With the memory switch off, the agent learns from only the step it just took, with no remembered steps.
312	  - Given memory off, when the agent learns, then each update uses exactly the latest step.
313	- **ABL-02** With the frozen-copy switch off, the agent builds its targets from its current value estimate.
314	  - Given frozen copy off, when a target is built, then it uses the current estimate's best next value, which changes after every update.
315	- **ABL-03** With the exploration-decay switch off, the agent's random-move probability stays fixed at 0.1 for the whole run.
316	  - Given decay off, when the agent picks moves at any point in training, then about 10% are random.
317	- **ABL-04** Each ablated agent is compared with the full agent of its environment (Snake: the value-learning agent; the tank: the limited-update agent) with the `null-hypothesis-testing` skill, given the final evaluation scores of both (5 training seeds each, on the same 100 held-out games); the ablation counts as hurting learning only if the full agent's scores are higher at a 5% significance level after correcting for the 3 comparisons in that environment.
318	  - Given finished runs of the full agent and the 3 ablated agents of one environment, when the study is run, then each ablation is reported as "hurts learning" or "no detectable difference", with its test result.
319	- **ABL-05** When no switch hurts learning, the study is still complete: the result is recorded as a finding in the notes, and S1 doesn't count as failed (nor S2, for the tank's switches).
320	  - Given a study report where all 3 ablations show "no detectable difference", when the stage is checked, then it passes, and the notes page records that no switch hurt learning as a finding (EXPL-02).
321	- **ABL-06** With the value-estimate switch off, the limited-update agent weights each move by the discounted return that followed it, as rung 1 does, and learns no value estimate.
322	  - Given value estimate off, when a fish's rewards were +1, +1, 0, then its moves are weighted 1.99, 1 and 0 before rescaling.
323	- **ABL-07** With the update-cap switch off, the limited-update agent still makes its 10 passes over each game's steps, with no limit on how far a move's probability can move.
324	  - Given cap off and a move already 1.3 × as likely as when played, with a positive advantage, when the next pass updates, then that move still becomes more likely.
325	- **ABL-08** With the rescaling switch off, the limited-update agent uses each game's advantages as built, without rescaling.
326	  - Given rescaling off, when a game's advantages are used, then they equal the advantages as built.
327	
328	**Edge cases & errors**
329	- Several switches are off in one run → allowed; the run's log records every switch's state.
330	- An ablated run fails before finishing → the study reports that ablation as missing, never as "no detectable difference".
331	- A switch is set on an agent that doesn't have that part (the tank's switches on rungs 1 or 2's missing pieces, Snake's switches on a tank rung) → the run is refused at start-up, naming the switch and the agent.
332	
333	### Baselines (`BASE`)
334	**Serves:** Goal 2
335	**Stage:** S1
336	**Behavior:** Two non-learning agents play the same seeded games as the learning agents in each environment: a random agent and a simple scripted agent. In the predator-prey tank each side has both, a random and a scripted predator and a random and a scripted prey. Their scores set the bars every learning agent's curve must clear.
337	
338	**Requirements**
339	- **BASE-01** The random agent picks each move uniformly at random from the allowed moves: one of the choices, or each control anywhere within its limits.
340	  - Given 10,000 moves by the random agent on Snake, when counted, then each of the 3 moves is picked 30–37% of the time.
341	  - Given 10,000 moves by the random agent in the tank, when counted, then turns are spread evenly over −15° to +15° and speeds over 0 to 0.3, each tenth of either range holding 9–11% of moves.
342	- **BASE-02** On Snake, the scripted agent moves toward the food along the axis with the larger gap, and when that move is immediately fatal it takes any non-fatal move, preferring straight.
343	  - Given food straight ahead and no danger ahead, when the scripted agent moves, then it goes straight.
344	  - Given food straight ahead and a wall ahead, when it moves, then it turns to a side that is free.
345	- **BASE-03** Both baselines are scored on the same 100 held-out games as the learning agent.
346	  - Given the 100 held-out seeds, when each baseline plays them, then each records one score per seed, and its mean and spread are logged.
347	- **BASE-04** In the tank, the scripted fish swims at full speed, turning as far as it can away from the ray that sees the ball closest; with no ball in reach it swims straight, and when the wall is within 1 unit straight ahead it turns as far as it can toward the side whose rays see more room.
348	  - Given the ball seen only on the front-left ray and no wall within 1 unit ahead, when the scripted fish moves, then it turns −15° (to the right) at speed 0.3.
349	  - Given no ball in reach and the wall 0.5 units straight ahead with more room on the left, when it moves, then it turns +15° at speed 0.3.
350	
351	- **BASE-05** The scripted predator swims at full speed, turning as far as it can toward the ray that sees a prey closest (not turning when that ray points straight ahead); with no prey in reach it swims straight, and when the wall is within 1 unit straight ahead it turns as far as it can toward the side whose rays see more room.
352	  - Given a prey seen only on the front-left ray, when the scripted predator moves, then it turns +10° (to the left) at speed 0.35.
353	  - Given no prey in reach and the wall 0.5 units straight ahead with more room on the right, when it moves, then it turns −10° at speed 0.35.
354	- **BASE-06** The scripted prey swims at full speed: when the wall is within 1 unit straight ahead it turns as far as it can toward the side whose rays see more room; otherwise, when the predator is in reach it turns as far as it can away from the ray that sees the predator closest; otherwise it turns as far as it can toward the ray that sees a pellet closest; with nothing in reach it swims straight.
355	  - Given the predator seen only on the front-left ray and no wall within 1 unit ahead, when the scripted prey moves, then it turns −15° at speed 0.3.
356	  - Given no predator in reach and a pellet seen only on the right ray, when it moves, then it turns −15° at speed 0.3.
357	
358	**Edge cases & errors**
359	- Every move is fatal → the scripted agent goes straight and the game ends.
360	- The scripted fish sees the ball equally close on two rays → it turns away from the one further left.
361	- The scripted fish sees the ball and the wall ahead at once → it avoids the wall.
362	- The scripted predator or prey sees its target equally close on two rays → it uses the one further left.
363	
364	### Evaluation (`EVAL`)
365	**Serves:** Goal 2
366	**Stage:** S1
367	**Behavior:** Every training run is checked at regular points on a fixed set of held-out games it never trains on, with exploration off, and ends with a final check on all 100 held-out games. Each learning agent's final check is compared with both baselines of its environment on those same games. In the predator-prey tank each side is checked against the other side's scripted opponent, and compared with the random and scripted versions of itself against that same opponent.
368	
369	**Requirements**
370	- **EVAL-01** Training games and evaluation games come from separate seed sets that never overlap.
371	  - Given any training run, when its training and evaluation seeds are listed, then no seed appears in both.
372	- **EVAL-02** During training the agent plays held-out games at regular points with exploration off and its mean score is logged: on Snake every 50 training games over all 100 held-out games; in the tank at every tenth of training over the same 20 of its 100 held-out games; in the predator-prey tank at every tenth of training, each side's current policy against the other side's scripted opponent over the same 20 of its 100 held-out games. Every run ends with a final check over all 100.
373	  - Given a Snake run of 1,000 training games, when it ends, then 20 evaluation results are logged, each over the same 100 seeds.
374	  - Given a tank run, when it ends, then 10 evaluation results over the same 20 seeds and one final result over all 100 seeds are logged.
375	  - Given a predator-prey run, when it ends, then for each side 10 evaluation results over the same 20 seeds and one final result over all 100 seeds are logged.
376	- **EVAL-03** Each learning agent's final evaluation scores are compared with each baseline's scores of its environment with the `null-hypothesis-testing` skill, given 5 training seeds × the 100 held-out games for the agent and the same 100 games for each baseline; the agent beats a baseline only if its scores are higher at a 5% significance level after correcting for every comparison in that environment (2 on Snake: the full agent against 2 baselines; 6 in the tank: 3 rungs against 2 baselines; 12 in the predator-prey tank: 3 rungs × 2 sides against 2 baselines, 8 without the taking-turns rung). In the predator-prey tank, each rung's last predator generation plays the scripted prey, and is compared with the random and the scripted predator playing the scripted prey on the same games; each rung's last prey generation is compared the same way against the scripted predator.
377	  - Given finished runs of the full agent and both baselines, when the comparison is run, then the report says, for the random agent and for the scripted agent, whether the agent beats it, with the test result.
378	  - Given finished runs of all 3 rungs and both tank baselines, when the comparison is run, then the report says, for each of the 6 pairs, whether the rung beats the baseline, with the test result.
379	  - Given finished runs of the self-play rung and both baselines of each side, when the comparison is run, then the report says, for the predator against the random and the scripted predator, and for the prey against the random and the scripted prey, whether the learned side beats it, with the test result.
380	- **EVAL-04** On Snake the full agent's learning curve beats both the random baseline and the scripted baseline; in the tank every rung's learning curve beats both the random fish and the scripted fish; in the predator-prey tank the self-play rung's predator beats both the random and the scripted predator, and its prey beats both the random and the scripted prey.
381	  - Given the EVAL-03 report for Snake, when it is read, then the agent beats both baselines.
382	  - Given the EVAL-03 report for the tank, when it is read, then each of the 3 rungs beats both baselines.
383	  - Given the EVAL-03 report for the predator-prey tank, when it is read, then the self-play rung's predator and prey each beat both baselines of their side.
384	
385	**Edge cases & errors**
386	- A run stops early → its completed evaluation points are kept and shown; it is left out of the final comparison, which names it as incomplete.
387	
388	### Run logging (`LOG`)
389	**Serves:** Goal 2, Goal 4
390	**Stage:** S1
391	**Behavior:** Every run, whether training or baseline, keeps a record of its settings and results, so curves can be redrawn and agents replayed without training again.
392	
393	**Requirements**
394	- **LOG-01** Each run records its environment, its agent (each side's, where two sides learn), every setting including the ablation switches and the rung, and its seed.
395	  - Given a finished run, when its record is opened, then the environment, the agent, every setting and the seed are listed.
396	- **LOG-02** Each run records the score and length of every training game, with each side's score where there are two.
397	  - Given a run of 1,000 games, when its record is read, then it holds 1,000 scores and game lengths.
398	  - Given a predator-prey run of 1,000 games, when its record is read, then it holds 1,000 predator scores, 1,000 prey scores and 1,000 game lengths.
399	- **LOG-03** Each run records every evaluation result and the training loss every 50 games.
400	  - Given a finished run, when its record is read, then each evaluation point has its mean score and spread, and each 50-game point has its average loss.
401	- **LOG-04** Each training run saves its final trained agent next to its record; a predator-prey run saves every generation of each side (SELF-02).
402	  - Given a finished training run, when its record is opened, then the saved agent can be loaded (AGENT-06).
403	  - Given a finished predator-prey run, when its record is opened, then each of its 20 saved generations can be loaded (SELF-07).
404	- **LOG-05** A run's record is kept on disk while the run is in progress, at least after every evaluation point.
405	  - Given a run stopped at game 430, when its record is opened, then results up to game 400 are present.
406	
407	**Edge cases & errors**
408	- Starting a run with the same name as an existing one → refused, naming the existing run; nothing is overwritten.
409	
410	### Learning curves (`CURVE`)
411	**Serves:** Goal 2, Goal 1
412	**Stage:** S1
413	**Behavior:** From saved run records, the learner draws learning curves: evaluation score against training games, with the environment's baselines as flat reference lines. Curves for several runs can share one chart, so the full agent and its ablations, or the three rungs of a ladder, can be compared at a glance. In the predator-prey tank each side has its own chart.
414	
415	**Requirements**
416	- **CURVE-01** A learning curve shows the mean evaluation score against training games for one or more runs.
417	  - Given one run's record, when its curve is drawn, then the chart shows one line of mean evaluation score at each evaluation point.
418	- **CURVE-02** Runs of the same agent and settings with different seeds are drawn as one line of their mean, with a band showing their spread.
419	  - Given 5 seeds of the full agent, when drawn, then the chart shows one mean line and one band.
420	- **CURVE-03** The random and scripted baselines' mean scores of the chart's environment appear on every chart as labelled flat lines.
421	  - Given any Snake chart, when drawn, then two labelled flat lines show the random and scripted baselines' mean scores.
422	  - Given any tank chart, when drawn, then two labelled flat lines show the random and scripted fish's mean scores.
423	  - Given a predator-prey chart of the predator, when drawn, then two labelled flat lines show the random and the scripted predator's mean scores against the scripted prey; a chart of the prey shows the random and the scripted prey's against the scripted predator.
424	- **CURVE-04** The full agent and each ablated agent can be drawn together on one chart, each labelled by its switches.
425	  - Given the full agent and 3 ablation runs, when drawn together, then 4 labelled lines appear.
426	- **CURVE-05** Curves are drawn from saved records only, never by training again.
427	  - Given saved records and no training running, when a chart is drawn, then it appears in ≤ 10 seconds.
428	- **CURVE-06** The 3 rungs of the ladder can be drawn together on one chart, each labelled by its rung.
429	  - Given 5 seeds of each rung, when drawn together, then 3 labelled mean lines with bands appear.
430	- **CURVE-07** The rungs of the self-play ladder can be drawn together, one chart per side, each line labelled by its rung.
431	  - Given 5 seeds of each of the 3 self-play rungs, when drawn, then a predator chart and a prey chart each show 3 labelled mean lines with bands.
432	
433	**Edge cases & errors**
434	- A record has no evaluation points yet → it is skipped with a note naming it; the other curves are drawn.
435	- Records from different environments are drawn together → refused, naming the environments.
436	
437	### Replay viewer (`REPLAY`)
438	**Serves:** Goal 3
439	**Stage:** S1
440	**Behavior:** The learner picks any saved agent, or a baseline, and a seed, and watches it play that game frame by frame in a simple view of the environment, with pause, single-step and speed controls.
441	
442	**Requirements**
443	- **REPLAY-01** The learner can watch any saved trained agent, the random agent or the scripted agent play a game from a chosen seed; in the predator-prey tank the learner picks one of these for each side, a saved generation counting as a saved agent.
444	  - Given a saved agent and seed 42, when a replay is requested, then the agent's game on seed 42 is shown from the first frame to game over.
445	  - Given predator generation 10, prey generation 3 and seed 42, when a replay is requested, then their game on seed 42 is shown from the first frame to game over.
446	- **REPLAY-02** Each frame shows the environment's drawing (CORE-04) plus the step number and score.
447	  - Given a Snake replay, when any frame is shown, then the grid, snake, food, step number and score are visible.
448	  - Given a tank replay, when any frame is shown, then the tank, the ball, each living fish with its heading, the number of fish left and the step number are visible.
449	  - Given a predator-prey replay, when any frame is shown, then the tank, the pellets, the predator with its heading, each living prey with its heading and energy, the number of prey left and the step number are visible.
450	- **REPLAY-03** The learner can pause, step one frame forward and change the playback speed between 1 and 30 frames per second.
451	  - Given a playing replay, when paused and stepped, then exactly one frame advances; when the speed is set to 30, then 30 frames show per second.
452	- **REPLAY-04** Replaying the same agent on the same seed shows the identical game every time.
453	  - Given a saved agent and seed 42, when replayed twice, then every frame and the final score match.
454	- **REPLAY-05** The final score of a replayed game matches that game's score in the agent's evaluation.
455	  - Given a held-out seed scored in evaluation, when it is replayed with the same saved agent, then the replay's final score equals the logged score.
456	
457	**Edge cases & errors**
458	- The saved agent doesn't exist or can't be loaded → an error names it; no window opens.
459	- The game never ends → it ends by the environment's limit (SNAKE-05, TANK-06), so every replay has a last frame.
460	
461	### Explanation notes (`EXPL`)
462	**Serves:** Goal 1
463	**Stage:** S1
464	**Behavior:** Each environment has a notes page, written by the learner in their own words, explaining why the agent learns, what each ablated part does on Snake and in the tank, in the tank what each rung of the ladder fixes, and in the predator-prey tank why self-play works and what each rung of its ladder shows, next to the evidence from that environment's study.
465	
466	**Requirements**
467	- **EXPL-01** Each environment has a notes page with a section explaining why the agent learns there.
468	  - Given Snake is finished, when its notes page is opened, then it has a "Why it learns" section written by the learner.
469	- **EXPL-02** The notes page has one section per ablation switch, giving that ablation's study result and the learner's explanation of it.
470	  - Given the ablation study report, when the notes page is read, then each of the 3 switches has a section with its result (ABL-04) and an explanation.
471	- **EXPL-03** The notes page includes the learning-curve charts comparing the full agent, its ablations and the baselines; in the tank also the 3 rungs and the baselines; in the predator-prey tank the rungs of each side against that side's baselines, and each rung's cross-play table.
472	  - Given the Snake notes page, when opened, then the CURVE-04 chart with the CURVE-03 baseline lines is shown on it.
473	  - Given the tank notes page, when opened, then the CURVE-04 chart and the CURVE-06 chart, each with the CURVE-03 baseline lines, are shown on it.
474	  - Given the predator-prey notes page, when opened, then the two CURVE-07 charts, each with the CURVE-03 baseline lines, and each rung's cross-play table (CROSS-02) are shown on it.
475	- **EXPL-04** The tank's notes page has one section per rung above the first, saying which failure of the rung below it fixes, with its ladder result (PG-10).
476	  - Given the ladder report, when the tank notes page is read, then rungs 2 and 3 each have a section with their result and the learner's explanation.
477	- **EXPL-05** The predator-prey notes page has a "Why self-play works" section and one section per rung, giving its baseline result (EVAL-03) and its cross-play result (CROSS-03), and saying whether the simultaneous learners cycled or stalled where self-play did not, or recording that they didn't.
478	  - Given the baseline and cross-play reports, when the predator-prey notes page is read, then it has a "Why self-play works" section, and each finished rung has a section with both results and the learner's explanation.
479	
480	**Edge cases & errors**
481	- An ablation shows no detectable difference → its section still exists and explains why that part may not matter in that environment.
482	- A rung shows no detectable difference from the one below → its section still exists and explains why the fix may not matter in the tank.
483	- The simultaneous learners show no cycling or stalling → the notes record that, with the cross-play table as evidence.
484	
485	### Discrete tank (`TWIN`)
486	**Serves:** Goal 1
487	**Stage:** S2
488	**Source:** [ideas/fish-tank.md](ideas/fish-tank.md)
489	**Behavior:** An optional twin of the fish tank where each fish picks one of 8 compass moves at full speed instead of steering continuously, trained by the unchanged value-learning agent, so the learner can see what continuous control adds. S2 is complete without it.
490	
491	**Requirements**
492	- **TWIN-01** The discrete tank is the fish tank with each fish choosing one of 8 compass directions (north, north-east, …, north-west) each step and swimming 0.3 units that way; everything else is as in the fish tank.
493	  - Given a fish at the centre, when it picks north-east, then it ends 0.3 units north-east of the centre, heading north-east.
494	- **TWIN-02** The value-learning agent trains on the discrete tank with no change to how it learns, treating each fish's steps as its own remembered steps.
495	  - Given the discrete tank with 5 fish, when the value-learning agent trains on it, then training completes, and its run is logged and drawn against the tank's baselines.
496	
497	**Edge cases & errors**
498	- The discrete tank is left unbuilt → nothing else in S2 depends on it.
499	
500	## Quality requirements
501	- **QR-01** Every training run of a learning agent finishes in under ~1 hour (≤ 60 minutes) on the learner's laptop CPU: the value-learning agent on Snake; each rung and ablation in the tank with 5 fish; and each rung in the predator-prey tank with 5 prey, where one run is the whole run, every generation of both sides.
502	  - Timed on the learner's laptop with no GPU, for the default number of training games.
503	- **QR-02** Each environment's full study runs unattended to completion with one command: Snake's (full agent and 3 ablations × 5 seeds, plus baselines) in ≤ 10 hours; the tank's (3 rungs and 3 ablations × 5 seeds, plus baselines) in ≤ 15 hours; the predator-prey tank's (3 rungs × 5 seeds, plus both sides' baselines and every run's cross-play table) in ≤ 16 hours, or ≤ 11 hours without the taking-turns rung.
504	  - Started once on the learner's laptop, it finishes without intervention; total time read from the run records.
505	- **QR-03** Every learning algorithm is written by hand: the value targets, remembered steps, frozen copy, exploration and update step of the value-learning agent, the move spreads, returns, value estimate, advantages, update cap and update step of the ladder, and the generation saving, frozen side and opponent mix of the self-play ladder; no reinforcement-learning library is used. General array math with automatic gradients is allowed.
506	  - Checked by reading the project's dependency list and the agents' code.
507	- **QR-04** The lab uses only free tools and needs no paid service or account.
508	  - Checked by reading the dependency list: every item is free to use.
509	- **QR-05** On the same machine, a training run repeated with the same seed and settings produces the same logged results.
510	  - Two runs of 200 games with seed 1 give identical evaluation scores.
511	
512	## Assumptions
513	- The learner is the only user; the lab runs on their laptop.
514	- Tiny test environments exist only to prove the shared core; they are not lab environments.
515	- Snake: 20 × 20 grid, starting length 3, step limit 100 × length, rewards +10 / −10 / 0.
516	- Value-learning agent settings: exploration 1.0 → 0.01 over half the games, memory of 100,000 steps, discount 0.9, frozen copy refreshed every 1,000 steps, fixed 0.1 exploration when decay is off.
517	- Study size: 5 training seeds per agent, 100 held-out games; Snake evaluates every 50 games, the tank at every tenth of training on 20 games plus a final 100; Snake's study ≤ 10 hours.
518	- The scripted baseline avoids immediately fatal moves; the agent may only tie it, and that bar is kept on purpose.
519	- Tank: radius 10, ball radius 1 at 0.25 per step, fish radius 0.3, 5 fish by default (1–10), 1,000-step limit; turn −15° to +15° then swim 0 to 0.3 per step.
520	- A fish senses 8 rays of reach 4 (wall, ball, nearest other fish) plus its own speed, and doesn't sense the ball's motion; the bet is that the rays are enough to dodge.
521	- Tank reward is +1 per step alive, with no extra penalty when hit; the scripted fish flees the ball and steers clear of the wall, a tougher bar kept on purpose.
522	- Ladder settings: discount 0.99, blend 0.95, one update per game, 10 passes in batches of 64, a 20% cap; the tank's switches apply to rung 3.
523	- Learning "more steadily" is reported as the spread across seeds, not tested; the tank's study is ≤ 15 hours.
524	- The discrete tank is optional; S2 is complete without it.
525	- Predator-prey tank: radius 10, 5 prey by default (1–10) moving as S2's fish, one predator of radius 0.5 turning −10° to +10° and swimming 0 to 0.35 per step (faster but less agile), catching at < 0.8; 1,000-step limit. Only prey have energy: 1.0 at the start, −0.002 per step, +0.3 per pellet up to 1.0. 10 pellets of radius 0.2 are always present, and a contested pellet goes to the closer prey.
526	- Prey see 34 numbers (wall, predator, nearest other prey and nearest pellet on 8 rays of reach 4, plus speed and energy); the predator sees 17 (wall and nearest prey on 8 rays, plus speed). Prey earn +1 per step alive; the predator +1 per catch.
527	- Self-play settings: 10 generations per side per run, one at every tenth of training; games are 50% current against current, 25% against a past prey generation and 25% against a past predator generation, each past one drawn uniformly. Taking turns is 10 alternating blocks, prey first, and is optional.
528	- Only the self-play rung must beat both baselines of each side and show later generations beating earlier ones; the other rungs are reported. The cross-play table is 10 × 10 over 20 held-out games; the predator-prey study is ≤ 16 hours, or ≤ 11 without taking turns.
529

180	- **Invariants:** positions in tank units, origin at the centre, y up, every fish within radius 9.7; `heading` in degrees anticlockwise from east; `fish` holds the living fish only; `survived` holds every fish's steps survived so far

196	`class LineEnv(Env): def __init__(self, points: int = 2, length: float = 5.0, max_steps: int = 50) -> None; name = "line"; obs_size = 1; moves = Controls(("push",), (-1.0,), (1.0,))`
197	- **Invariants:** point ids `0..points-1`, each starting at 0 and observing its own position / `length`; each step a point moves by its push, held at 0 from below; a point reaching `length` gets +1 and is removed (CORE-08), every other step gives 0; the game ends when every point is removed or after `max_steps`; score is the number of points that reached the end (CORE-07)
