  "content": "# RL Lab — Architecture\n\n**Status:** Draft · **Shape:** Single package · **Last updated:** 2026-10-19 · **Spec:** [spec.md](spec.md)\n\n## Overview\nOne Python package, `rllab`. Every environment and every agent sits behind the protocols in `core`. An environment lists its sides, each with its own observation size, its moves (a set of choices or continuous controls with limits) and its agent ids (AD-11, AD-19). It takes one move per living id and reports a result for each, with a score per side. Snake and the corridor have one side with one agent. The fish tank and the line have one side with several agents, which can be removed mid-game. The predator-prey tank and the chase test environment have two sides, each played by its own agent. The training loop, evaluation, run records, curves and replay viewer use only those protocols, so they never name an environment. A training run plays seeded games with hand-written learners: the DQN learns after every step, and the three policy-gradient rungs learn once after every game (AD-12). In a two-sided run a schedule picks each game's match: which agent plays each side and which sides learn, so the predator-prey tank's three ways of training (taking turns, learning at once and self-play against past generations) are three schedules over one loop, and the run saves 10 generations of each side as it goes (AD-20, AD-22). The run scores each learning side greedily on held-out seeds at regular points and once more at the end, against the other side's scripted opponent where there is one, and rewrites its JSON record after each check. The cross-play table replays every saved predator generation against every prey generation from the saved files (AD-23). The study command trains every variant of an environment's study plan, 5 seeds each, scores the baselines and builds the cross-play tables. It then runs the `null-hypothesis-testing` comparisons (against the baselines, up the tank's ladder, for each ablation, and each side's last generation against its first) and writes a report, charts and tables for the learner's notes page. The notes command reads that page and reports missing sections and broken links; no code ever writes it (AD-10). Charts and tables are drawn only from saved records. A replay re-plays saved agents on a seed, so it never depends on frames saved during training.\n\n```mermaid\nflowchart LR\n  cli --> study --> train\n  cli --> train\n  cli --> replay\n  cli --> curves\n  cli --> crossplay\n  study --> curves\n  study --> crossplay\n  train --> evaluate\n  train --> runs\n  study --> evaluate\n  replay --> evaluate\n  replay --> registry\n  crossplay --> registry\n  crossplay --> evaluate\n  study --> registry\n  cli --> registry\n  registry --> snake & corridor & tank & hunt & line & chase & agent & pg & baselines & selfplay\n  tank & hunt --> arena\n  snake & corridor & tank & hunt & line & chase & agent & pg & baselines & evaluate & selfplay -.->|protocols| core\n  curves --> runs\n  crossplay --> runs\n```\n\n## Tech stack\n| Area | Choice | Decision |\n|---|---|---|\n| Language | Python 3.12 | AD-01 |\n| Networks and gradients | PyTorch 2.14, CPU wheels; `torch.nn.Linear`, autograd and `torch.optim.Adam` only | AD-02, AD-03, AD-13, AD-17 |\n| Numerics and both tanks' physics | NumPy ≥ 2.0 | AD-02, AD-14, AD-21 |\n| Run records | JSON (stdlib) plus a `torch.save` state dict per side and generation | AD-05, AD-15 |\n| Learning curves | Matplotlib 3.9, Agg backend, PNG | AD-07 |\n| Cross-play tables | Markdown written by `crossplay` | AD-23 |\n| Replay viewer | pygame-ce 2.5 | AD-06 |\n| Study parallelism | `concurrent.futures.ProcessPoolExecutor` (stdlib) | AD-03 |\n| CLI | `argparse` (stdlib) | — |\n| Tests | pytest | — |\n\nEvery item is free and open source, and none needs an account (QR-04).\n\n## Components\n| Component | Responsibility | Covers | Depends on |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| `core` | `Env`, `Agent`, `LearningAgent` and `Schedule` protocols; the `Side`, move, `Lineup`, `Match`, `Step`, `Frame` and `Transition` types; the domain errors; start-up checks of an environment and of a lineup against it | CORE-01–CORE-04, CORE-08, CORE-10 | — |\n| `arena` | The geometry both tanks share: turn-then-swim with a wall stop, wall and circle ray readings, and random start spots, as NumPy functions over arrays of fish | TANK-02, TANK-07, TANK-08, HUNT-02, HUNT-03, HUNT-08, HUNT-09 | — |\n| `snake` | Classic Snake on a 20 × 20 grid: rules, the 11 facts, rewards, step limit and frame drawing | SNAKE-01–SNAKE-08 | `core` |\n| `tank` | The fish tank: the ball's bounces, removals, the fish's 25 numbers, rewards, time limit and frame drawing, plus its 8-move compass twin | TANK-01–TANK-10, TWIN-01 | `core`, `arena` |\n| `hunt` | The predator-prey tank: the predator's catches, pellets and their respawn, prey energy and starving, both sides' observations, rewards and scores, time limit and frame drawing | HUNT-01–HUNT-12 | `core`, `arena` |\n| `corridor` | The 1 × 5 corridor test environment that proves the core works for an environment other than Snake | CORE-05, CORE-06 | `core` |\n| `line` | The 2-point continuous test environment that proves the core works with continuous controls, several agents and removals | CORE-07, CORE-08 | `core` |\n| `chase` | The two-sided chaser-and-runner test environment that proves the core trains two sides, each learning or frozen | CORE-10 | `core` |\n| `agent` | Hand-written DQN: value network, remembered steps, frozen copy, exploration schedule, update step, the 3 Snake ablation switches, save and load | AGENT-01–AGENT-06, ABL-01–ABL-03, TWIN-02, QR-03 | `core` |\n| `pg` | Hand-written policy-gradient ladder: policy spreads, returns, value estimate, advantages, rescaling, update cap, the 3 tank ablation switches, save and load of a side's agent or generation | PG-01–PG-09, PG-12, ABL-06–ABL-08, SELF-01, SELF-07, QR-03 | `core` |\n| `baselines` | Random agent for choices or controls; the scripted Snake agent that reads the true game state; the scripted fish, predator and prey that read their rays | BASE-01, BASE-02, BASE-04–BASE-06 | `core`, `snake` |\n| `evaluate` | Disjoint training and held-out seed sets; plays one game, asking only living agents, each through its side's agent; scores one side greedily on a seed set | EVAL-01, EVAL-02, BASE-03, AGENT-03, PG-02, CORE-08 | `core` |\n| `selfplay` | The self-play ladder's schedules: taking turns, learning at once, and self-play with the opponent mix and the pool of past generations | SELF-03–SELF-05, QR-03 | `core` |\n| `runs` | Run records on disk, one folder per environment: create without overwriting, append, flush after each evaluation point and generation, save each side's agent and generations, load | LOG-01–LOG-05, SELF-02 | `agent`, `pg`, `selfplay`, `evaluate` |\n| `train` | Training loop: each game's match, games, per-step and per-game learning for learning sides only, evaluation points per side and the final check, generations, logging, early-stop errors | CORE-05, CORE-07–CORE-10, AGENT-02, EVAL-02, PG-04, SELF-02, QR-01, QR-05 | `core`, `evaluate`, `runs` |\n| `registry` | Turns names into objects: environment names into environments; a run config into its learners, evaluation opponents and schedule; `random`, `scripted`, a run folder or a generation into one side's agent; an environment into its evaluation cadence | CORE-06, CORE-07, CORE-10, REPLAY-01, EVAL-02, SELF-01, SELF-07 | `snake`, `tank`, `hunt`, `corridor`, `line`, `chase`, `agent`, `pg`, `baselines`, `selfplay`, `runs` |\n| `curves` | Learning-curve charts from saved records, one side per chart: seed means and bands, baseline lines, ablation and rung labels | CURVE-01–CURVE-07, EXPL-03 | `runs` |\n| `replay` | Re-plays one agent per side on a seed into frames, and shows them in a window with pause, step and 1–30 fps controls | REPLAY-01–REPLAY-05 | `core`, `evaluate`, `registry` |\n| `crossplay` | The cross-play table: every saved generation of one side against every one of the other on 20 held-out games, saved per run, and each rung's table as Markdown | CROSS-01, CROSS-02 | `core`, `evaluate`, `runs`, `registry` |\n| `study` | Each environment's full study as one command: its variants × 5 seeds, the baselines and the cross-play tables, then the baseline, ladder, ablation and generation comparisons run with the **`null-hypothesis-testing`** skill; writes the report, charts and tables for the notes page, and checks the learner's notes page without writing it (AD-10) | ABL-04, ABL-05, PG-10, PG-11, EVAL-03, EVAL-04, CROSS-03, CROSS-04, SELF-06, SELF-08, EXPL-01–EXPL-05, QR-02 | `agent`, `pg`, `selfplay`, `train`, `evaluate`, `runs`, `registry`, `curves`, `crossplay` |\n| `cli` | Commands: `python -m rllab` with `train`, `baselines`, `study`, `curves`, `replay`, `crossplay` or `notes` | QR-02 | all of the above |\n\nEvery dependency points one way. `core` and `arena` import nothing internal, and only `tank` and `hunt` import `arena`. Only `registry`, `baselines` and `cli` may import an environment component (`snake`, `tank`, `hunt`, `corridor`, `line`, `chase`), and `baselines` imports only `snake`, since the scripted fish, predator and prey read only their observations. Only `registry` and `study` construct learners, and `train` and `selfplay` import neither learner: `selfplay` loads past generations through the loader `registry` hands it. Import-linter enforces these rules (`build-setup`). The seam for later: `core`, `evaluate`, `runs`, `train`, `selfplay`, `curves` and `replay` never name an environment or a learner, so they could move out together as a shared package.\n\n## Interfaces\nSee [packages/rllab/contract.md](packages/rllab/contract.md).\n\n## Data\n- **Seeds (AD-04):** training game seeds are drawn from `[0, 1_000_000)` by a generator built from the run seed. The 100 held-out seeds are fixed at `1_000_000–1_000_099`, and regular evaluation points and the cross-play table use the first 20 where the environment's cadence says so. The two ranges can't overlap (EVAL-01), and every agent and baseline of an environment is scored on the same held-out games (BASE-03, EVAL-03).\n- **Run folder (AD-05, AD-15):** `runs/<env>/<name>/record.json` holds the config with each side's agent, one score per side and a length per training game, the evaluation points (per learning side: mean, spread and per-seed scores), the final check per side over all 100 held-out seeds, the average loss per side per evaluation window, the saved generation numbers, the status and the elapsed time. Next to it, `<side>.pt` holds each learning side's state dict, sizes and config. A run with generations keeps them as `generations/<side>-<NN>.pt`, 10 per side in the predator-prey tank, and its cross-play table as `crossplay.json` (AD-23). One-sided environments' baselines get `runs/<env>/baseline-random/` and `runs/<env>/baseline-scripted/`; a two-sided environment's get `baseline-random-<side>/` (that side random, every other side scripted) and `baseline-scripted/` (every side scripted) (AD-24). Study runs are named `<variant>-s<seed>`, for example `runs/snake/no-memory-s3`, `runs/tank/no-cap-s2` or `runs/hunt/selfplay-s4`.\n- **Study output:** `runs/<env>/study/report.md` (every comparison with its test result, each learner's spread across seeds, missing or incomplete runs, and the finding lines ABL-05, PG-11 and SELF-08 ask for), with its charts: `curves.png` (the full agent, its ablations and the baselines) in Snake and the tank, `ladder.png` (the 3 rungs and the baselines) in the tank, and `curves-predator.png` and `curves-prey.png` (each side's rungs and its baselines) in the predator-prey tank, which also gets one `crossplay-<rung>.md` per rung. The learner's own `notes/<env>.md` (EXPL-01–EXPL-05) links them. The code reads the notes page (`study.check_notes`) but never writes it, so the learner's words are never overwritten (AD-10).\n\n## Cross-cutting concerns\n- **Errors:** the domain errors are in `core`: `InvalidMoveError`, `WrongAgentsError`, `GameOverError` and `MissingSideError` (ValueErrors), `EnvCheckError` and `MoveKindError` (TypeErrors), and `NonFiniteLossError`; `runs.RunExistsError` and `pg.PGConfig`'s switch refusal join them. `train` catches failures during the run, marks the record `failed` with the game and step, flushes it with the generations saved so far and re-raises, so the log so far survives.\n- **Speed (QR-01, QR-02):** each run uses one CPU thread (`torch.set_num_threads(1)`). The study runs up to `os.cpu_count() - 1` runs, and then the cross-play tables, at once in separate processes (AD-03). The default of 1,000 training games per run, in Snake, in the 5-fish tank and in the 5-prey predator-prey tank, is checked against the 60-minute limit by a timing test on the learner's laptop; in the predator-prey tank one run is the whole run, every generation of both sides.\n- **Reproducibility (QR-05, SNAKE-08, TANK-10, HUNT-12, REPLAY-04):** no global random state anywhere. Each environment, agent, schedule and run owns a `np.random.Generator` or `torch.Generator` built from its seed. Living agents are always asked, and learn, in id order. Deterministic torch algorithms and a single thread give identical logged results on the same machine (AD-04).\n- **Hand-written learning (QR-03):** `agent` and `pg` may import `torch` and `numpy` only, and `selfplay` `numpy` only; generation saving lives in `train` and `runs`. No gymnasium, stable-baselines, PettingZoo or other RL library is a dependency, and a dependency check enforces it (`build-setup`).\n- **Testing:** the corridor proves the core is environment-agnostic (CORE-05, CORE-06), the line proves it with continuous controls, several agents and removals (CORE-07, CORE-08), and the chase proves it with two sides, each learning or frozen (CORE-10). The Snake, tank and predator-prey rules are tested from seeded fixed-move games, and both tanks' also from hand-placed states (`reset_to`, AD-18); a rung's arithmetic is tested through `pg`'s pure functions (AD-18), and a schedule's matches through `selfplay.make_schedule` alone. CORE-09 is a golden case (`test-planning`): S1's exit records a 200-game Snake run with seed 1 and S2's a 200-game rung-3 tank run with seed 1, and every later milestone that touches `core`, `evaluate`, `runs` or `train` repeats both and compares every logged evaluation score.\n\n## Project layout\n```\nsrc/rllab/       → core/ arena/ snake/ tank/ hunt/ corridor/ line/ chase/ agent/ pg/ baselines/ evaluate/ selfplay/ runs/ train/ registry/ curves/ replay/ crossplay/ study/ cli/ — one folder per component; __init__ exports its contract\nruns/            → run records and study output, one folder per environment (git-ignored)\nnotes/           → the learner's notes pages, one per environment\ntests/           → folders only; files are planned in pipelines.md\n```\n\n## Decisions\n### AD-01: Python 3.12, one package `rllab`\n**Status:** Accepted · **Date:** 2026-10-18 · **Scope:** system\n- **Context:** The user said \"Single package, go with your recommendation.\" (UR-28). This is research code with one user, one laptop and one install (brief Constraints). The user already knows Python, NumPy and PyTorch (UR-07).\n- **Decision:** One package, `rllab`, on Python 3.12, with one folder per component.\n- **Alternatives:** Several packages (core, envs, agents): no separate install, release or runtime justifies the split, and the core's seam is already named. Python 3.13: PyTorch CPU wheels and pygame-ce lag on new versions.\n- **Consequences:** Set `requires-python = \">=3.12,<3.13\"`. A later split moves the environment-agnostic folders as they are, with their contract sections.\n\n### AD-02: A hand-written DQN on the 11 facts; PyTorch only for the network and its gradients\n**Status:** Accepted · **Date:** 2026-10-18 · **Scope:** system\n- **Context:** The user called Snake a \"Good first env for DQN-style value learning\" (UR-06) and said \"I want to write DQN from scratch\" (UR-08). QR-03 rules out RL libraries but allows array math with automatic gradients. AGENT-01–AGENT-05 fix the algorithm: a learned value per move, ε-greedy decaying 1.0 → 0.01 over the first half of the games, 100,000 remembered steps, discount 0.9, and a frozen copy refreshed every 1,000 steps.\n- **Decision:** `agent.DQNAgent` is written by hand: the replay memory (a NumPy ring buffer), the ε schedule, the target computation, the frozen-copy refresh, the Huber-loss update and the ablation switches. Only the value network uses PyTorch: an MLP of observation size → 256 → move count (11 → 256 → 3 on Snake) built from `torch.nn.Linear`, trained by autograd and `torch.optim.Adam` (lr 1e-3, batch 64). It makes one update per remembered step once a full batch is remembered: one per environment step on Snake, one per fish-step in the compass tank (TWIN-02). AD-17 sets the PyTorch version.\n- **Alternatives:** NumPy only, with backprop written by hand: it teaches more about gradients, but that isn't the lesson the brief wants and it adds bug surface; it could come later as an exercise. A Q-table (2¹¹ × 3 entries): it would learn Snake well, but it isn't DQN, and with no frozen copy or replay memory to switch off, the ablations would mean nothing.\n- **Consequences:** The networks are the only library-backed parts, so the \"from scratch\" check (QR-03) reduces to reading `agent/`, `pg/` and `selfplay/`. The network size, learning rate, batch size and Huber loss are choices the spec doesn't fix, and they live in `AgentConfig` with these defaults.\n\n### AD-03: CPU only; one thread per run, separate processes for the study\n**Status:** Accepted · **Date:** 2026-10-18 · **Scope:** system\n- **Context:** The user's words are \"Laptop CPU only\" (UR-13). QR-01 limits one run to ≤ 60 minutes, and QR-02 limits each environment's study. Networks this small gain nothing from torch's thread pool, and extra threads hurt reproducibility (QR-05).\n- **Decision:** Install PyTorch from the CPU wheel index; the code never calls CUDA or MPS. Each run calls `torch.set_num_threads(1)`. `study` spreads its runs, and any per-run work after training, over `ProcessPoolExecutor(max_workers=os.cpu_count() - 1)`, with each job in its own process and writing its own folder.\n- **Alternatives:** Running a study serially: 15 to 30 runs at up to 60 minutes each could exceed the limits. Batching several agents into one process (vectorised): faster, but it tangles the hand-written agents and makes ablations harder to read.\n- **Consequences:** A run's time doesn't depend on the other runs, as long as there are enough cores. On a 4-core laptop, 3 runs go at once: Snake's 20 runs take about 7 rounds within 10 hours, so each run needs to be under about 80 minutes when 3 run in parallel; the tank's 30 take 10 rounds within 15 hours, about 90 minutes each; AD-24 sets the predator-prey tank's. The timing test measures one run alone and 3 in parallel.\n\n### AD-04: Seeds owned per object, fixed held-out range\n**Status:** Accepted · **Date:** 2026-10-18 · **Scope:** system\n- **Context:** SNAKE-08, TANK-10, HUNT-12, CORE-01, AGENT-06, PG-12, REPLAY-04, REPLAY-05 and QR-05 need same seed → same game. EVAL-01 needs the training and held-out seeds never to overlap.\n- **Decision:** `Env.reset(seed)` builds the environment's generator from the game seed. `Agent.reset(seed)` reseeds any randomness the agent uses while playing (the random baseline). Every other object that draws at random during a run builds its generators from the run seed: a learner's exploration, sampling and weight initialisation, and the schedule's draws (AD-22). The held-out seeds are `evaluate.EVAL_SEEDS = range(1_000_000, 1_000_100)`, and training seeds come from `[0, 1_000_000)`. Deterministic torch algorithms are switched on in every run.\n- **Alternatives:** One global seed set at start-up: any extra random call anywhere would shift every later game. Hashing training seeds: overlap with the held-out seeds would become unlikely rather than impossible.\n- **Consequences:** A replay of a saved agent on a held-out seed reproduces its logged evaluation score (REPLAY-05). Results are reproducible on one machine, not across machines (QR-05 asks only for the first).\n\n### AD-05: Run records as one JSON file per run, rewritten atomically\n**Status:** Accepted · **Date:** 2026-10-18 · **Scope:** system\n- **Context:** LOG-01–LOG-04 decide what a record holds, and LOG-05 needs it on disk at least after every evaluation point. At 1,000 games with 20 evaluation points of 100 scores, a record stays well under 1 MB, and two sides' scores keep it there. CURVE-05 needs charts in ≤ 10 seconds from records alone.\n- **Decision:** `runs.RunWriter` keeps the record in memory. After every evaluation point and every saved generation, and when the run finishes or fails, it rewrites `record.json` through a temporary file and `os.replace`. Each learning side's agent is saved as `<side>.pt` (state dict, observation size, moves, config), and each generation as `generations/<side>-<NN>.pt`, at the paths `runs.agent_path` names. Creating a run whose folder already exists raises `RunExistsError` (LOG edge case). AD-15 sets where the folders go.\n- **Alternatives:** SQLite: queryable, but one file per run is simpler to read, diff and delete. CSV per game plus JSON settings: two formats to keep in sync. Parquet: adds pyarrow to read a few kB.\n- **Consequences:** A crash loses at most the games since the last evaluation point, which LOG-05 allows, and never a saved generation (SELF edge case). Reading a record means reading the whole file, which is fine at this size.\n\n### AD-06: The environment draws a toolkit-free `Frame`; the viewer is pygame-ce\n**Status:** Accepted · **Date:** 2026-10-18 · **Scope:** system\n- **Context:** The user chose \"Go with pygame-ce for the replay viewer.\" (UR-29). CORE-04 has every environment draw its own frame: a grid for Snake, and a round tank with heading fish for both tanks. REPLAY-03 needs pause, single step and 1–30 fps, and CORE-06 needs the viewer to work with any environment.\n- **Decision:** `Env.render()` returns a `core.Frame`: a grid of cell codes with a colour for each code, then circles and line segments drawn over it in cell units, plus the step number, each side's score and a caption. Environments never import a GUI toolkit. `replay.show` draws frames in a pygame-ce window: space pauses, → steps one frame, and ↑/↓ change the speed between 1 and 30 fps. Its clock limits the frame rate.\n- **Alternatives:** A Matplotlib animation: no extra dependency, but timing at 30 fps and keyboard control are unreliable across backends. A Tkinter canvas: in the standard library, but the event loop and timing take more code. A terminal view: no colour grid on every terminal. Rasterising the tanks to cells only: a fish's heading would be lost at any readable grid size.\n- **Consequences:** One extra dependency, used only by `replay`. Frames are plain data, so replay tests check frames without opening a window.\n\n### AD-07: Learning curves with Matplotlib from saved records\n**Status:** Accepted · **Date:** 2026-10-18 · **Scope:** system\n- **Context:** CURVE-01–CURVE-07 need one or more runs as lines, seed bands, baseline lines and labels for each ablation and rung, drawn from records only, one chart per side where there are two. The scope rules out every other view but the cross-play table (UR-18). EXPL-03 puts the charts on the notes page.\n- **Decision:** `curves.plot_curves` reads `RunRecord`s, takes one side's evaluation results, and groups them by config minus seed. It draws each group's mean line with a min–max band, and each baseline as a labelled flat line. It saves a PNG with the Agg backend.\n- **Alternatives:** Plotly HTML: interactive, but not needed, and a PNG embeds cleanly in a Markdown notes page. Seaborn: one more dependency to draw a mean and a band.\n- **Consequences:** Charts are static PNGs. The band is min–max over the 5 seeds; the spread on evaluation points is the standard deviation over the held-out games.\n\n### AD-08: The environment is passed to `Agent.act`, so the scripted Snake baseline reads the true Snake state\n**Status:** Accepted · **Date:** 2026-10-18 · **Scope:** system\n- **Context:** BASE-02 moves along the axis with the larger gap to the food. The 11 facts say which way the food is but not how far, so the scripted agent needs the game state. The training loop, evaluation and replay must still treat every agent the same way (CORE-05, CORE-06).\n- **Decision:** `Agent.act(obs, env, *, explore)` receives the environment it is playing. `baselines.ScriptedAgent` requires a `snake.SnakeEnv` and reads `env.state`; every other agent ignores `env`, apart from checking which environment and side it was given.\n- **Alternatives:** Adding food distances to the observation: changes SNAKE-06's 11 facts. An `info` dict on every step: untyped, and every environment would have to fill it.\n- **Consequences:** The scripted Snake agent is Snake-only. Each environment's scripted baseline is its own class per side (the scripted fish, predator and prey read only their rays, BASE-04–BASE-06), and `registry` resolves `scripted` to the one for the environment and side, refusing one that has none and naming it.\n\n### AD-09: The comparisons use each learner's finished seeds; only an ablation or a rung with a failed seed is missing\n**Status:** Accepted · **Date:** 2026-10-18 · **Scope:** system\n- **Context:** test-planning found that `study.compare_scores` required the full agent to be 5 × 100, while the spec's EVAL edge case leaves a stopped run out of the final comparison and names it incomplete, and `run_study` fails only when the full agent has no finished run. A study with 4 finished full-agent runs had no valid call. The ABL edge case says an ablation with a failed run is reported as missing (TC-10).\n- **Decision:** `compare_scores` takes `full` and `other` with 1–5 rows each (one per finished training seed) and 100 columns in `EVAL_SEEDS` order, and pairs by held-out game on each side's per-game mean over its rows. Against the baselines, each learner uses its finished runs, names the rest in `incomplete`, and is `missing` only when none finished; `run_study` raises only when the study's full agent has none. An ablation with any unfinished seed is `missing`, never compared on fewer seeds, and so is a ladder comparison in which either rung has one; their runs are named in `incomplete`. `Comparison.method` states how many seeds each side used.\n- **Alternatives:** Failing the whole study on any stopped full-agent run: breaks the EVAL edge case and loses hours of finished runs (QR-02). Comparing an ablation or a rung on its finished seeds too: the ABL edge case says missing, and fewer seeds weaken a \"no detectable difference\" verdict without saying so.\n- **Consequences:** A study with a stopped learner run still compares it with the baselines, with less power, and the report says how many seeds were used. Pairing per game on seed means matches TC-08's test, so the `null-hypothesis-testing` design holds.\n\n### AD-10: The notes page is the learner's; the code checks it and never writes it\n**Status:** Accepted · **Date:** 2026-10-18 · **Scope:** system\n- **Context:** EXPL-01, EXPL-02, EXPL-04 and EXPL-05 ask for explanations written by the learner, so generating the page would defeat them, yet nothing showed whether the page meets EXPL-01–EXPL-05. PL-06 adds a check the learner runs.\n- **Decision:** `study.check_notes` reads `notes/<env>.md` and the study's `report.md` and returns a `NotesCheck`: missing or empty required sections, and links to the report, charts and tables that don't resolve. No function in `rllab` opens a notes page for writing; a missing page is reported, never created.\n- **Alternatives:** Writing a template page for the learner to fill: saves typing, but risks overwriting their words and makes an unfilled template look done. No check: EXPL-01–EXPL-05 would be judged by eye only.\n- **Consequences:** EXPL-01–EXPL-05 are covered by a check of the learner's page, not by generated text. The check judges structure (headings present and non-empty, links resolve), never what the learner wrote.\n\n### AD-11: One core for every environment: a move per living agent, as choices or controls\n**Status:** Accepted · **Date:** 2026-10-18 · **Scope:** system\n- **Context:** The user wants the tank to \"reuse the shared core\" (UR-49) and said \"Yes, the core can grow as long as Snake runs unchanged.\" (UR-56). Asked whether to grow it now or in S2, they chose \"(a), change it now.\" (UR-59): nothing is built yet. CORE-02, CORE-03 and CORE-07 need one loop for one agent or several and for a few choices or continuous controls; CORE-08 needs removed agents dropped mid-game; TANK-04 makes the fish independent copies of one problem.\n- **Decision:** `core.Env.reset` returns one observation per agent id, and `step` takes a `Mapping[int, Move]` with one move per living agent and returns a `Step` with per-agent observations and rewards and the set still alive. Each side's moves (AD-19) are `Choices(n)` or `Controls(names, low, high)`, and a `Move` is an `int` or a float array to match. Every `Agent` declares the kind it plays (`choices`, `controls` or `any`), and `core.check_lineup` refuses a mismatch at start-up. One agent object is asked once per living id of its side, so all fish of a side share one policy (PG-04). Snake and the corridor are one-agent environments with id 0 and `Choices`.\n- **Alternatives:** Keeping S1's single-agent, integer-move core and adding a multi-agent protocol, an adapter and second train, evaluate and replay entry points in S2 (option b): no rework of M1's approved plan, but two parallel sets of entry points to keep in step. A vectorised step taking an array of moves for all agents: faster in the tank, but removed fish leave holes in the array and Snake gains a batch dimension for nothing.\n- **Consequences:** Snake's code carries a one-entry dict at each step, and its results are what they would have been (CORE-09). AD-19 adds sides on top of this rule, and the docs written against the core go back through pipeline-design, test-planning and build-planning before the build starts.\n\n### AD-12: Learners learn per agent-step, per game, or both\n**Status:** Accepted · **Date:** 2026-10-18 · **Scope:** system\n- **Context:** AGENT-04 has the DQN learn from remembered steps as it plays; PG-05 has every rung learn once after each game from all its fish's steps. CORE-05 and CORE-07 need one training loop for both, and TWIN-02 has the DQN treat each fish's steps as its own.\n- **Decision:** `core.LearningAgent` has `learn(t)`, called with each living agent's `Transition` of its side, in id order, after every step, and `end_game()`, called once after every training game; each returns its update's loss or `None`. Both are called only in games where its side learns (AD-20). The DQN updates in `learn` and returns `None` from `end_game`; a rung only stores the step in `learn` and updates in `end_game`. `Transition.agent` names the fish, so a rung computes each fish's returns up to that fish's end. `train` logs, per side, the mean of every loss returned in each evaluation window.\n- **Alternatives:** A per-game learner handed the whole game's trajectories by the loop: `train` would keep two code paths and buffer the steps itself. Separate training loops for value and policy learners: breaks CORE-07's \"no changes to the core\".\n- **Consequences:** The loop is the same for every learner, and the DQN's calls on Snake are exactly the single-agent loop's. A rung holds one game's steps in memory, at most 10 fish × 1,000 steps.\n\n### AD-13: The policy-gradient ladder: one hand-written `PGAgent` with a rung number and 3 switches\n**Status:** Accepted · **Date:** 2026-10-18 · **Scope:** system\n- **Context:** The user wants \"policy-gradient/PPO and continuous control\" (UR-48) and chose \"The full ladder with off switches.\" (UR-55). PG-01–PG-09 fix the mechanics: a centre and width per control, clipping to the limits, one update per game from every fish's steps at discount 0.99, an advantage blend of 0.95, rescaled advantages on rungs 2 and 3, and on rung 3 10 passes in batches of 64 with a 20% cap. ABL-06–ABL-08 switch off rung 3's value estimate, cap and rescaling. QR-03 rules out RL libraries.\n- **Decision:** `pg.PGAgent` takes `PGConfig.rung`. The policy is an MLP of observation size → 64 → 64 → one centre per control (tanh hidden layers) with a learned, observation-independent log width per control. Moves are drawn from a normal distribution and clipped to the limits, and log-probabilities are taken on the clipped move. Rung 1 weights each move's log-probability by the discounted return to that fish's end and makes one Adam step per game. Rung 2 adds a value MLP of the same size, trained by mean-squared error on the returns, and weights moves by the 0.95-blended advantage, rescaled. Rung 3 adds 10 passes over the game's steps in shuffled batches of 64, with the probability ratio clipped to 0.8–1.2. With `value` off, rung 3 uses the returns in place of advantages; with `cap` off, the ratio isn't clipped; with `rescale` off, advantages are used as built. Adam lr 3e-4. A game ended by the time limit stops returns at step 1,000 with no value carried past it.\n- **Alternatives:** Three separate agent classes: the switches would cut across class lines, and the \"switch on a rung without that part\" check would be scattered. A tanh-squashed policy instead of clipping: an exact log-probability, but PG-03 describes clipping a draw that falls outside, and squashing changes what \"centre\" means in PG-01 and PG-02. Width predicted from the observation: more expressive, but less stable for a first policy-gradient build.\n- **Consequences:** The module code can still read rung by rung (one file per rung's addition, laid out by `pipeline-design`). Hidden sizes, lr, the log-width start and clipping before the log-probability are choices the spec doesn't fix, kept in `PGConfig`. Taking log-probabilities on the clipped move biases the gradient when the policy pushes past a limit (see Risks).\n\n### AD-14: The tank is simulated in NumPy, in continuous tank units\n**Status:** Accepted · **Date:** 2026-10-18 · **Scope:** system\n- **Context:** TANK-01–TANK-10 fix the geometry, motion, sight, rewards and limits: radius 10, a ball of radius 1 at 0.25 per step with mirror bounces, fish of radius 0.3 steering −15° to +15° and swimming 0 to 0.3, removal when centres are closer than 1.3, 8 rays of reach 4, and a 1,000-step limit. QR-01 needs a 5-fish run of 1,000 games in under an hour, so each step must be cheap. TWIN-01 adds a compass twin.\n- **Decision:** `tank.TankEnv` keeps positions in float64 tank units around the centre. Each step: turn and swim every living fish (stopping at the wall), move the ball and reflect its velocity about the wall's normal on contact, then remove every fish within 1.3 of the ball. The swimming, the wall stop, the rays (against the wall circle, the ball and each other fish as circles, scaled by the reach, for all living fish at once) and the start spots are `arena`'s NumPy functions, shared with the predator-prey tank (AD-21). The game seed builds a `np.random.Generator` for the ball's direction and the fish's spots and headings. The compass twin is the same class with `compass=True`: `Choices(8)`, the heading set to the chosen direction, speed 0.3.\n- **Alternatives:** A physics library (pymunk, Box2D): collisions here are circle-against-circle and circle-in-circle, so a library adds a dependency and its own time-stepping for a few lines of geometry. A grid tank: breaks continuous movement (TANK-02). A separate class for the twin: duplicates every rule TWIN-01 says is shared.\n- **Consequences:** The tank's rules are plain arithmetic, tested from seeded fixed-move games and from hand-placed states started with `reset_to` (AD-18). Bounces are computed at the step's end position, so the ball can sit up to 0.25 inside the wall's contact point for one step; TANK-01's angle cases hold.\n\n### AD-15: Run folders grouped by environment; regular checks on a subset, a final check on all 100\n**Status:** Accepted · **Date:** 2026-10-18 · **Scope:** system\n- **Context:** Every environment writes baselines and study runs: one `runs/baseline-random` would be every environment's, and the compass tank's DQN runs would take Snake's `full-s<seed>` names. EVAL-02 checks Snake every 50 games on all 100 held-out seeds, and the tank and each side of the predator-prey tank at every tenth of training on the same 20 seeds, and has every run end with a final check on all 100. EVAL-03, ABL-04 and PG-10 compare final scores.\n- **Decision:** `runs.create_run` writes `root/<env>/<name>/`; the compass tank's runs share `runs/tank/` with the continuous tank's, so they are drawn against the same baselines (TWIN-02). `RunConfig` carries `eval_every` and `eval_games`, filled from the `EvalSchedule` that `registry.eval_schedule` returns, and `RunRecord.final` holds each scored side's final check over all 100 seeds. When the last regular point falls on the last game over all 100 seeds (Snake), it is the final check; otherwise `train` plays the final check after the last game. The study compares `final` scores.\n- **Alternatives:** Putting the environment in every run name: longer names, and the baselines' folders still need a rule. Re-running all 100 seeds for Snake's final check: the same scores at extra cost, since evaluation is deterministic.\n- **Consequences:** A Snake record's evaluation points are unchanged by the final check (CORE-09). Charts and the study find an environment's baselines in its own folder. Paths in commands become `runs/<env>/<name>`.\n\n### AD-16: Each environment's study is a `StudyPlan`; corrections apply per family\n**Status:** Accepted · **Date:** 2026-10-18 · **Scope:** system\n- **Context:** QR-02 asks for one unattended study per environment. The spec corrects each family of comparisons within itself: an environment's baseline comparisons (EVAL-03), a ladder's rung-against-rung comparisons (PG-10), its ablations (ABL-04) and its generation comparisons (CROSS-03). PG-10 also asks for each rung's spread across seeds, and ABL-05, PG-11 and SELF-08 make a missed bar a finding rather than a failure.\n- **Decision:** `study.STUDIES` maps each studied environment to a `StudyPlan`: its full agent, its learners in rung order, its ablations, a `Variant` per name, its scored sides, whether its learners form a ladder, which learners are optional, whether it builds cross-play tables, and the notes sections it requires. `run_study` trains every variant × seed, scores the baselines, and runs each family of one-sided `null-hypothesis-testing` comparisons, corrected within itself: every learner and side against each baseline; each rung against the one below, in a ladder; each ablation against the full agent; and each side's last generation against its first, with cross-play. A skipped optional learner leaves every family and table. The report adds each learner's spread across seeds and the finding lines. `check_notes` takes its required sections from the same plan.\n- **Alternatives:** Correcting over every comparison in the study at once: stricter than the spec asks and different per environment. A separate ladder command: a second unattended run for the tank, against QR-02's one command.\n- **Consequences:** An environment joins by adding a `StudyPlan`; each plan's variants and counts are in `study.StudyPlan` and in its stage's decision (AD-24 for the predator-prey tank).\n\n### AD-17: PyTorch 2.14\n**Status:** Accepted · **Date:** 2026-10-18 · **Scope:** system\n- **Context:** pip-audit reported findings against torch 2.5.1, and the user chose \"For torch, take your recommendation (a).\" (UR-45): move to a current torch rather than keep 2.5 under an exception. PyPI's release history (https://pypi.org/project/torch/#history, read 2026-10-18) lists 2.14.1, released 2026-09-30, with CPython 3.12 wheels for Linux, Windows and macOS 14+ on Apple silicon. AD-02 and AD-13 use only `nn.Linear`, autograd, Adam, `torch.save` and `torch.load`, `set_num_threads` and deterministic algorithms, which 2.14 keeps.\n- **Decision:** Depend on `torch>=2.14.1,<2.15`, CPU wheels (AD-03). Every saved agent stores tensors and plain Python values only (the config as a dict), so it loads with `torch.load`'s default `weights_only=True`.\n- **Alternatives:** Staying on 2.5 with exception EX-02: the user declined it. Leaving the version open (`>=2.14`): a later minor could change determinism or `torch.load`, and QR-05 depends on both.\n- **Consequences:** `pyproject.toml` and the lock move to 2.14 through `build-setup`. The macOS wheel needs macOS 14 or later on the learner's laptop.\n\n### AD-18: Test seams: the tanks start from a hand-placed state, and a rung's arithmetic is public\n**Status:** Accepted · **Date:** 2026-10-18 · **Scope:** system\n- **Context:** The S2 golden cases check the spec's own numbers: a bounce at 30°, a fish 0.1 from the wall, a ray reading 0.5 (TC-18–TC-21), a draw clipped from +20° to +15° (TC-22), returns of [1.99, 1, 0] and an advantage of −2 (TC-24), and a cap gradient of 0 (TC-25). The predator-prey tank's cases do the same: a prey 0.7 from the predator, energy 0.5 beside a pellet, two prey at one pellet. Seeded games can't reach those states, and `PGAgent`'s methods only expose the result of a whole update, so each case needs a seam the contract names.\n- **Decision:** `tank.TankEnv.reset_to(state)` and `hunt.HuntEnv.reset_to(state)` start a game from a hand-placed state and return the observations; `reset(seed)` and `reset_to` share every rule after the start. `pg` exports its arithmetic as pure functions, and `PGAgent` is built from them: `draw` and `clip_move` for moves, `discounted_returns`, `advantages` and `rescale` per fish or game, `step_weights` choosing among them by `PGConfig` rung and switches, and `capped_objective` per move in torch, so its gradient can be checked. The functions take plain arrays, never `Transition`s.\n- **Alternatives:** Testing only through `PGAgent` with a recorded game: the spec's numbers would sit behind a network and an optimizer, and a wrong sign could pass. Private helpers imported by the tests: tests would bind to the module layout, which `pipeline-design` may move.\n- **Consequences:** `pg` exports 7 functions beside `PGAgent`, and each tank environment one extra method; neither interface is frozen yet, so no built code changes.\n- **Probe:** none — no external API, vendor or dataset\n\n### AD-19: Each environment lists its sides; agents are given per side\n**Status:** Proposed · **Date:** 2026-10-19 · **Scope:** system\n- **Context:** CORE-02, CORE-03 and CORE-10 add environments with two sides that see and move differently: the predator sees 17 numbers and turns ±10° at up to 0.35, the prey see 34 and move as the tank's fish. AD-11's core had one observation size and one set of moves per environment, and one agent per game. The user said \"reopen the core contract if you need to.\" (UR-69). The CORE edge case refuses a lineup that leaves a side without an agent, naming it, and CORE-09 keeps Snake's and the tank's results.\n- **Decision:** `core.Env.sides` replaces `obs_size` and `moves`: a tuple of `core.Side(name, obs_size, moves, ids)`, with disjoint ids. Snake (`snake`), the corridor (`walker`), the tank (`fish`) and the line (`point`) have one side; the predator-prey tank has `predator` (id 0) and `prey` (ids 1–n), and the chase `chaser` and `runner`. Every function that plays a game takes a `core.Lineup`, one agent per side name, or a lone `Agent` on a one-sided environment; `core.check_lineup` normalises it and refuses a missing side (`MissingSideError`) or a kind mismatch, replacing `check_agent`. Each id is played by its side's agent, so one object still plays every fish of a side (PG-04). Scores are per side (`core.Scores`) in `Step`, `Frame`, `GameResult` and the run records; an `EvalResult` is one side's, and `evaluate` names the side it scores.\n- **Alternatives:** A composite agent that routes ids to two policies inside one `Agent`: no core change, but one `obs_size` and one `moves` can't describe two sides, so start-up checks and loading couldn't check either side. A separate two-sided protocol and loop beside the one-sided ones: Snake's code would stay as written, but train, evaluate, replay and curves would each keep two entry points, the reason AD-11 rejected the same split.\n- **Consequences:** Nothing is built yet (M1 has 0 of 22 tasks done), so the cost is docs: M1's approved plan, PL-01–PL-06 and the S1 and S2 golden cases are written against the old signatures and go back through pipeline-design, test-planning and build-planning before the build starts. A one-sided caller still passes a lone agent, and its results are unchanged (CORE-09).\n- **Probe:** none — no external API, vendor or dataset\n\n### AD-20: Two-sided training: a schedule picks each game's match, and train saves generations\n**Status:** Proposed · **Date:** 2026-10-19 · **Scope:** system\n- **Context:** CORE-10 has each side learn or play frozen in any game; SELF-03–SELF-05 set three ways to choose, SELF-02 saves 10 generations per side at every tenth of training, and QR-03 has the generation saving, the frozen side and the opponent mix written by hand. CORE-05 and CORE-07 keep one training loop for every environment. EVAL-02 checks each side against the other side's scripted opponent.\n- **Decision:** `train.train_run` takes the learners per side, the evaluation opponents per side and an optional `core.Schedule`. Before each game it asks `schedule.match(game)` for a `core.Match`: the agent playing each side and the sides that learn. Only learning sides' learners get `begin_game`, `learn` and `end_game`; a frozen side's agent only acts, drawing moves as in training (`explore=True`). With no schedule every learner plays and learns, which is every one-sided run. Every `n_games / generations` games, train saves each learning side's agent as a generation through `RunWriter.save_generation` and hands the file to `schedule.add_generation`. Each evaluation point scores each learning side against the opponents of the others, and losses are logged per side. `selfplay` builds the schedules (AD-22), and `registry.make_run` assembles a run's learners, opponents and schedule from its `RunConfig`.\n- **Alternatives:** A second loop for two-sided runs: breaks CORE-10's \"no changes to the core\" and duplicates evaluation and logging. A frozen flag on the learner (`agent.frozen = True`): every learner would carry the switch, and a past generation, which isn't the learner, would still need another path.\n- **Consequences:** Snake's and the tank's runs take the no-schedule path, so their calls are as before (CORE-09). A schedule can pause a side's learning for whole blocks without the learner knowing. A past generation is a loaded agent that train never teaches, so it can't change (SELF-05).\n- **Probe:** none — no external API, vendor or dataset\n\n### AD-21: The predator-prey tank reuses the tank's geometry; energy is kept in thousandths\n**Status:** Proposed · **Date:** 2026-10-19 · **Scope:** system\n- **Context:** The user said \"Reuse S2's tank and hand-written PPO for both sides.\" (UR-73). HUNT-01–HUNT-12 keep the tank's radius, controls, wall stop, pass-through and 8 rays of reach 4, and add a predator, 10 respawning pellets and prey energy (1.0 at the start, −0.002 per step, +0.3 per pellet up to 1.0). HUNT-05 removes a prey that never eats at exactly step 500, and HUNT-06 expects 0.798 and 0.998; 500 float64 subtractions of 0.002 don't land exactly on 0. The predator-prey golden cases need hand-placed states, as the tank's do (AD-18).\n- **Decision:** The tank's swimming, wall stop, ray casting and random placement live in `arena`, pure NumPy functions over arrays of fish, which `tank.TankEnv` and `hunt.HuntEnv` both call; `arena.FishState` is shared. `HuntEnv` runs each step in HUNT's order: swim (the predator's centre held within 9.5, the prey's within 9.7), catch (closer than 0.8), eat (within 0.5; the closer prey wins, the lower id on a tie; the pellet respawns by `arena.random_spots` at least 0.5 from every living prey), then energy. Energy is an integer in thousandths (1,000 at the start, −2 per step, +300 per pellet capped at 1,000) and is observed as thousandths / 1,000. The game seed builds the generator for every start spot, heading and respawn.\n- **Alternatives:** A predator mode inside `TankEnv`, as the compass twin is: the ball, pellets, energy and two sides would make one class serve two different games. `hunt` importing `tank`'s helpers: one environment depending on another, against the rule that only `registry`, `baselines` and `cli` import environments. Float energy with a tolerance: every edge at 0 and at 1.0 would need the same epsilon in the environment, the observation and the tests.\n- **Consequences:** The tank's rules don't change, only where its geometry lives (AD-14), so its golden cases hold. Energy readings are multiples of 0.001.\n- **Probe:** none — no external API, vendor or dataset\n\n### AD-22: The self-play ladder: one rung-3 `PGAgent` per side, three schedules\n**Status:** Proposed · **Date:** 2026-10-19 · **Scope:** system\n- **Context:** UR-73: \"Reuse S2's tank and hand-written PPO for both sides.\" SELF-01 trains both sides with the limited-update agent (PG-08), one policy per side. SELF-03 alternates 10 blocks, prey first; SELF-04 has both learn after every game; SELF-05 draws each game as current against current (50%), the current predator against a past prey (25%) or a past predator against the current prey (25%), the past generation uniform over those saved so far. SELF-07 loads a saved generation back, and QR-05 needs the same seed to give the same run.\n- **Decision:** Each side's learner is an unchanged `pg.PGAgent` with `PGConfig(rung=3)` and its side's controls; the prey's one agent plays every prey id (PG-04). `selfplay.make_schedule` builds the rung's `Schedule`: `turns` gives block `g · 10 // n_games` to one side, the prey first, the other playing its current agent frozen; `simultaneous` plays and teaches both every game; `selfplay` draws each game's kind, and a past generation, from a generator built from the run seed, and plays current against current until the opponent has a generation. A past generation is loaded once from its saved file with `PGAgent.load` and cached, and plays frozen, drawing moves from a generator seeded by the run seed and its generation number. `selfplay` gets the loader from `registry` and never imports `pg`.\n- **Alternatives:** Past generations playing greedy (centre moves): no generator needed, but the learner would train against a narrower opponent than the one it met when that generation was current. Copies held in memory instead of loaded files: no disk reads, but the opponent might not be the generation the cross-play table and the replays load (SELF-07). A learning rate or network size per side: the spec doesn't ask, and the rung-3 defaults are the ones the tank tests.\n- **Consequences:** The predator's policy is 17 → 64 → 64 → 2 and the prey's 34 → 64 → 64 → 2. A taking-turns side spends half the run frozen, so each side learns from 500 games of a 1,000-game run.\n- **Probe:** none — no external API, vendor or dataset\n\n### AD-23: The cross-play table is built from saved generations after training\n**Status:** Proposed · **Date:** 2026-10-19 · **Scope:** system\n- **Context:** CROSS-01 plays every predator generation against every prey generation on the same 20 held-out games with exploration off, for each finished run; CROSS-02 shows one table per rung, the mean over its 5 seeds, from saved generations only; CROSS-03 compares each side's last generation with its first on its scores against all 10 opponent generations × 20 games × 5 seeds, corrected for 6 comparisons (4 without taking turns), and the CROSS edge case leaves a run with fewer than 10 generations out, naming it. The scope allows the table as a view, and no other (UR-18).\n- **Decision:** `crossplay.build_table(run)` loads each saved generation through `registry.load_agent`, plays the 100 pairings on the first 20 `EVAL_SEEDS` with `evaluate.play_game`, and saves both sides' per-game scores as `crossplay.json` in the run folder. `crossplay.write_table` writes each rung's table as Markdown, `predator mean / prey mean` per cell over its runs, for the report and the notes page. `study.compare_generations` takes one side's last-generation and first-generation arrays (run × opponent generation × game, rows paired by run) and runs the **`null-hypothesis-testing`** comparison; a run missing generations is left out and named in `incomplete`, and the rest are compared.\n- **Alternatives:** Playing the table during training: later generations wouldn't exist yet, and it would add to QR-01's hour. A PNG heatmap: a picture of 200 numbers, and the scope rules out heatmaps; a Markdown table embeds in the notes page and diffs. Saving only each cell's means: CROSS-03 needs per-game scores to pair.\n- **Consequences:** Each run's table costs 2,000 greedy games, played in the study's pool after training (QR-02). A table can be rebuilt from the saved files at any time, never by training.\n- **Probe:** none — no external API, vendor or dataset\n\n### AD-24: The predator-prey study: three rungs, per-side baselines and a cross-play family\n**Status:** Proposed · **Date:** 2026-10-19 · **Scope:** system\n- **Context:** QR-02 runs the predator-prey study as one command: 3 rungs × 5 seeds, both sides' baselines and every run's cross-play table, in ≤ 16 hours, or ≤ 11 hours without taking turns, which SELF-06 makes optional. EVAL-03 compares each rung's last predator generation with the random and the scripted predator, all playing the scripted prey, and each rung's last prey generation the same way against the scripted predator: 12 comparisons, 8 without taking turns. The S3 rungs aren't compared with the rung below, and SELF-08 makes a taking-turns or simultaneous rung that misses a bar a finding. The predator-prey tank has no ablations (§Out of scope), and CURVE-07 draws one chart per side.\n- **Decision:** `STUDIES[\"hunt\"]` lists `turns`, `simultaneous` and `selfplay` (the full agent), each with both sides at rung 3, its `SelfPlayConfig`, 10 generations and 5 prey; it has no ladder family and no ablations, has a cross-play family, and makes `turns` optional (`run_study(\"hunt\", skip=(\"turns\",))`). A two-sided environment's baseline records are `baseline-random-<side>` (that side random, every other side scripted) and `baseline-scripted` (every side scripted), so the scripted pair is played once for both sides' bars: three records in the predator-prey tank. The report holds 12 (or 8) baseline and 6 (or 4) cross-play comparisons, the finding lines SELF-08 asks for, `curves-predator.png`, `curves-prey.png` and one `crossplay-<rung>.md` per rung.\n- **Alternatives:** Treating the rungs as a ladder, each against the one below: the spec doesn't ask, and two more comparisons in a family would add nothing to its bars. Four baseline records, random and scripted per side: the scripted pair would be played twice with identical results.\n- **Consequences:** On a 4-core laptop the 15 runs take 5 rounds of 3, and the 10 without taking turns 4 rounds, leaving about 3.2 hours per round within 16 hours (2.75 within 11) for a run of up to 60 minutes and its share of the cross-play games.\n- **Probe:** none — no external API, vendor or dataset\n\n## Risks and open questions\n- **Training time (QR-01, QR-02):** no default of 1,000 games has been timed. In Snake, a greedy agent that circles until the step limit makes evaluation games long; in the tank, a good policy plays up to 1,000 steps × 5 fish per game, each fish asking the policy network once per step, plus 10 evaluation points of 20 games and a final check of 100. A predator-prey run plays up to 6 fish per step, makes two rung-3 updates per learning game, evaluates both sides (twice the evaluation games), and saves 20 generations; its cross-play table then adds 2,000 greedy games per run inside the study's limit. Mitigation: the first milestone of each stage times one run alone and 3 in parallel, and S3's also times one cross-play table. If too slow, lower the default game count; the spec doesn't fix it.\n- **Settings the spec leaves open:** the DQN's 11 → 256 → 3 network, Adam lr 1e-3, batch 64, Huber loss and linear ε decay (AD-02), and the ladder's 64-unit networks, lr 3e-4 and log-width start (AD-13), used for both predator-prey sides (AD-22), are tutorial defaults, not tested here. If a learner doesn't beat the scripted baseline (EVAL-04), tune these before questioning the design.\n- **Frozen opponents draw moves (AD-20, AD-22):** a frozen side or past generation plays its policy's draws, not its centre. If self-play's later generations fail to beat earlier ones (CROSS-04) while the cross-play table, which is greedy, shows them close, try greedy opponents before questioning the ladder.\n- **Clipped log-probabilities (AD-13):** a policy whose centre sits past a limit (a fish always turning 15°) gets biased gradients. If curves stall with the centre outside the limits, take the log-probability of the unclipped draw instead and record it in `Transition`.\n- **Rays and one ball or one predator (brief bets):** the S2 idea bets 8 short rays carry enough to dodge the ball, and that fish don't sense its motion (UR-58); the S3 idea bets the same rays carry over to both predator-prey sides and that simultaneous learners show the moving-target failure in a small tank. If no rung beats the scripted fish, or the simultaneous learners don't cycle or stall, that is a finding for the notes (PG-11, SELF-08, EXPL-05), not a design failure; CROSS-04 is the one S3 bar on learning against past generations.\n- **Comparison method:** the test statistics, how the seeds × games (or runs × opponent generations × games) are paired and the multiple-comparison corrections are left to the `null-hypothesis-testing` skill when `study.compare_scores` and `study.compare_generations` are built. The contract passes it the raw per-seed, per-game scores so the skill can pair them; each side may have fewer than 5 seeds (AD-09).\n- **Docs to rework (AD-19, AD-20):** the grown core changes signatures that M1's approved plan, PL-01–PL-06 and the S1 and S2 golden cases were written against (`Env.sides`, lineups, per-side scores and records, `check_lineup`, `train_run`'s learners, opponents and schedule, `load_agent`'s side). docs/architecture.md:173:- **Decision:** `core.Env.reset` returns one observation per agent id, and `step` takes a `Mapping[int, Move]` with one move per living agent and returns a `Step` with per-agent observations and rewards and the set still alive. Each side's moves (AD-19) are `Choices(n)` or `Controls(names, low, high)`, and a `Move` is an `int` or a float array to match. Every `Agent` declares the kind it plays (`choices`, `controls` or `any`), and `core.check_agent` refuses a mismatch at start-up. One agent object is asked once per living id of its side, so all fish of a side share one policy (PG-04). Snake and the corridor are one-agent environments with id 0 and `Choices`.
docs/architecture.md:230:- **Decision:** `core.Env.sides` replaces `obs_size` and `moves`: a tuple of `core.Side(name, obs_size, moves, ids)`, with disjoint ids. Snake (`snake`), the corridor (`walker`), the tank (`fish`) and the line (`point`) have one side; the predator-prey tank has `predator` (id 0) and `prey` (ids 1–n), and the chase `chaser` and `runner`. Every function that plays a game takes a `core.Lineup`, one agent per side name, or a lone `Agent` on a one-sided environment; `core.check_agent` now takes the lineup, normalises it and refuses a missing side (`MissingSideError`) or a kind mismatch. Each id is played by its side's agent, so one object still plays every fish of a side (PG-04). Scores are per side (`core.Scores`) in `Step`, `Frame`, `GameResult` and the run records; an `EvalResult` is one side's, and `evaluate` names the side it scores.

D804sayI've drafted the S3 architecture extension, and it's back with you for review. full text
I've drafted the S3 architecture extension, and it's back with you for review. Your "Approved, looks good" came before there was anything to approve: the architecture was still the S2 version, and the S3 spec you approved left 29 requirements uncovered and changed 18 more.

**Files (both now Draft):** `docs/architecture.md` and `docs/packages/rllab/contract.md`. Every spec requirement now has a home, no requests are open, and lint shows 0 errors. The 4 warnings belong to other docs (`requests.md`, `tests.md`, `explained/`).

**Shape:** still a single package. No reason to split it came up: S3 adds no new library, and nothing runs on its own.

**What changed**
- **The core now has "sides" (AD-19).** Each environment lists its sides, and each side has its own observation size, moves and agent ids. Snake, the corridor, the tank and the line have one side each; the predator-prey tank (`predator` and `prey`) and the chase test environment have two. Games are played by a lineup (one agent per side), and scores, records and evaluations are kept per side. One-sided callers still pass a single agent.
- **A schedule decides each training game (AD-20).** Before each game, a schedule picks which agent plays each side and which sides learn. A frozen side only plays. Training saves a generation of each learning side at every tenth of training.
- **New components:**
  - `arena`: the tank's swimming, ray and start-spot code, now shared by both tanks.
  - `hunt`: the predator-prey tank.
  - `chase`: the two-sided test environment that proves the core.
  - `selfplay`: the three schedules (taking turns, learning at once, self-play).
  - `crossplay`: the cross-play table.
- **Components that grew:** `baselines` gains the scripted predator and prey. `runs` saves agents per side plus generations. `registry` gains `make_run` and generation specs (`runs/hunt/selfplay-s1@10`). `curves` draws one side per chart. `replay` takes one agent per side. `study` gains the predator-prey plan and `compare_generations`.
- **Decisions needing attention (all Proposed):**
  - AD-19: core sides.
  - AD-20: schedules and generations.
  - AD-21: the predator-prey tank reuses the tank's geometry, and energy is stored as whole thousandths, so a prey that never eats starves at exactly step 500.
  - AD-22: one unchanged rung-3 PPO agent per side, plus the three schedules.
  - AD-23: the cross-play table is built from saved generations after training, as Markdown tables.
  - AD-24: the predator-prey study, with three baseline records and the optional taking-turns rung skipped by `skip=("turns",)`.
- **Older decisions corrected in place:** AD-03, 04, 05, 06, 07, 08, 10, 11, 12, 14, 15, 16 and 18. None was superseded.
- **Biggest cost:** M1's plan, PL-01–PL-06 and the S1/S2 golden cases go back through pipeline-design, test-planning and build-planning. Nothing is built yet, so only docs change.

**Your words:**
- "reopen the core contract if you need to." UR-69 → AD-19 "`core.Env.sides` replaces `obs_size` and `moves`"
- "Reuse S2's tank and hand-written PPO for both sides." UR-73 → AD-21 "the tank's swimming, wall stop, ray casting and random placement live in `arena` … which `tank.TankEnv` and `hunt.HuntEnv` both call", and AD-22 "Each side's learner is an unchanged `pg.PGAgent` with `PGConfig(rung=3)`"
- Changes proposed: none. Open: none.

**Assumptions (what I chose)**
1. Side names: `snake`, `walker` (corridor), `fish`, `point` (line), `predator` (id 0) with `prey` (ids 1–n), and `chaser` with `runner`.
2. `core.check_agent` keeps its name but now takes a lineup and returns one. Renaming it would break references in the tool-written history in `routes.md`.
3. A frozen side or past generation draws moves from its policy, as it did in training, rather than playing greedy. The cross-play table and all evaluations play greedy. This is listed under Risks.
4. Past generations are loaded from their saved files and cached, with draws seeded by the run seed and generation number.
5. The scripted predator chases a prey it can see before checking the wall, and turns +10° for a prey directly behind it. The scripted prey follows the spec's order: wall, then predator, then pellet.
6. Two-sided baselines are three records: `baseline-random-predator`, `baseline-random-prey` and `baseline-scripted`. The scripted pair is played once and serves both sides' bars.
7. Cross-play tables are written as Markdown (`crossplay-<rung>.md`), and per-game scores are saved as `crossplay.json` in each run folder.
8. Saved agents are `<side>.pt`, so `runs/snake/full-s1/snake.pt` replaces `agent.pt`, and generations are `generations/<side>-NN.pt`.
9. In the prey's frame, energy is drawn as a bar of length 0.6 × energy under each prey.

**Open questions:** none blocking.

Approve, or tell me what to change. Once you approve, I'll hand the core changes to pipeline-design.
